`README.md`:

```md
<div align="center">

# IDA Better Reverse

**Name the libc inside a stripped static binary, explain every call in the pseudocode, and read any function as a flowchart you can zoom into, call after call.**

[![Release](https://img.shields.io/github/v/release/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse?color=4c1)](https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/latest)
[![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-blue)](LICENSE)
![IDA Pro 7.4–9.x](https://img.shields.io/badge/IDA%20Pro-7.4%E2%80%939.x-555)
![x86-64 · i386 · AArch64](https://img.shields.io/badge/arch-x86--64%20%C2%B7%20i386%20%C2%B7%20AArch64-555)

English · [简体中文](README.zh-CN.md)

</div>

<picture>
  <source srcset="docs/demo.avif" type="image/avif">
  <img src="docs/demo-poster.png" alt="IDA Better Reverse identifying glibc 2.39 in a stripped static binary, naming its functions and annotating the pseudocode" width="100%">
</picture>
<p align="center"><sub>IDA Pro 9.1, stripped static x86-64 binary, one click (recorded with v2.2.0). <a href="https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/download/v2.2.0/demo.mp4">MP4, 1080p60</a> · Data flow and flowcharts have <a href="#data-flow-and-flowcharts">their own video</a>.</sub></p>

IDA Better Reverse is an IDA Pro plugin for the binaries CTF reversing and pwn challenges, and
a lot of Linux malware, are made of: stripped and statically linked. It finds out which C
runtime build was linked in (glibc or musl, down to the release), names the library functions
with FLIRT signatures made for that exact build and for the GCC runtime next to it, and writes
what each libc call and system call does next to it in the Hex-Rays output. Then it helps read
the program's own code: one variable's data flow on its own, and the whole function as a
textbook flowchart in which a call opens into the called function's chart as you zoom into it.
It is plain Python with its data included; the entry file also runs as a command-line tool
without IDA.

## Names and notes

### Names the libc, from the bytes

A stripped static binary calls `sub_4776D0` where the source called `printf`. **Detect** ranks
glibc and musl builds by how many of their functions' byte fingerprints occur in the binary;
**Run** applies FLIRT signatures generated for the winning build and for its GCC runtime, and
gives the named functions their prototypes. On Butterfly: glibc 2.39, 838 functions named,
143 prototypes, one click.

<p align="center"><img src="docs/img/detect.png" alt="Detection result: glibc 2.39, confidence high, static x86-64, ranked against 2.40-2.43" width="46%"></p>

### Explains every call

A decompiler hook writes, next to each call, what it does with the values it is actually given:
`open` flags, `mmap` protections (RWX flagged), signals, `ptrace` requests, socket types, printf
and scanf conversions bound to their arguments, `syscall(n)` with the call named per
architecture. MMX/SSE/AVX intrinsic chains are folded into one plain expression with the lane
rules that make them differ from scalar code. The notes are drawn as the function is printed and
are not stored in the database.

<p align="center"><img src="docs/img/notes.png" alt="Butterfly's encoding loop: _m_pxor/_m_por/_m_paddb chains noted as *v17 ^ v24[0], rotl(v18, 8) (swaps the two bytes of every 16-bit lane), rotl(v19, 1) + v24[0] (+ per byte, no carry between bytes); printf calls with their arguments bound" width="100%"></p>

## Data flow and flowcharts

<picture>
  <source srcset="docs/flow.avif" type="image/avif">
  <img src="docs/flow-poster.png" alt="A key's data flow, the function as a flowchart, then three calls of xrtFuze opened one inside another by zooming" width="100%">
</picture>
<p align="center"><sub>IDA Pro 9.1, real recording: Butterfly (QWB 2025), then three calls deep in xrtFuze (QWB 2023). <a href="https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/download/v2.4.0/flow.mp4">MP4, 1080p60</a></sub></p>

### Follows one variable

**Ctrl-Alt-F** on a variable (or a global) opens a window with only the lines its value comes
from (▲), the lines that read or write it (●) and the lines it goes to (▼), inside the
if/loop headers around them; everything else is hidden. It follows values through memory:
reaching definitions over the function's control flow, points-to per object, pointer copies,
and calls that write through their arguments (`fread`, `memcpy`, an unknown `encrypt(buf, out)`).
Double-click a variable to follow it instead; Esc goes back.

<p align="center"><img src="docs/img/dataflow.png" alt="Data flow of v23 in main: the key loaded, copied to v24, used by the MMX loop, handed to sub_401CA0" width="80%"></p>

### Draws the function as a flowchart

**Space** turns the window into the whole function drawn the way textbooks draw a program:
statements in order, conditions as diamonds with yes/no exits, input and output as
parallelograms, calls of the program's own functions with double sides, loops back on the
left and out on the right, error branches in a column of their own. Arithmetic is written as
math (`v19 <<< 1`, `(per byte)`), not as intrinsics. The followed variable is marked yellow.
What it is computed from is purple (▲) and what is computed from it blue (▼), each only on the
lines where it takes part: the sources on its line and above, what it goes into below. The
lines that have nothing to do with it are faded, and a legend says which is which. Click a step
to light the path that leads to it.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/img/flowchart.png" alt="Flowchart of Butterfly's main with the key v23: v24, v18, v19 and the buffer pointers marked as computed from it" width="49%">
  <img src="docs/img/path.png" alt="A click on the success print: only the conditions and exits that lead there stay lit" width="49%">
</p>

### Opens calls as you zoom in

Every call of one of the program's own functions carries a ⊕. Rest the mouse on it and a small
picture of the called function's chart appears. Zoom into the call and that chart opens under
it, drawn to fit; zoom further and the calls inside open the same way, as deep as you go. A
breadcrumb in the legend says where you are. When the followed value is handed to the call,
the argument it arrives in is followed inside. Nothing is decompiled until a call is zoomed
into, one function per turn of the event loop, and a chart is dropped again when you zoom out.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/img/preview.png" alt="Resting on sub_804ADE9: a picture of its chart, zoom in to open" width="100%">
</p>
<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/img/nested.png" alt="Two calls down in xrtFuze: v8 = sub_804AB46(*ptr) opening under its call" width="49%">
  <img src="docs/img/inside.png" alt="Three calls down: main › sub_804ADE9 › sub_804ACDF › sub_804AB46, the flag check" width="49%">
</p>

## Evaluation

All numbers were measured with IDA Pro 9.1 (idalib and GUI) on an AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395.

### Function naming against ground truth

Two small C programs were built for x86-64, i386 and AArch64 with Ubuntu 24.04's GCC 13.3
against glibc 2.39 using `-static`, and stripped. Signatures were applied to the stripped binary, again until a pass
named nothing new; every name was checked against all symbols at that address in the
unstripped build (aliases such as `read`/`__read`/`__libc_read` count). Coverage is correct
names over all functions in the unstripped symbol table, precision is correct over named.

| Binary (functions) | IDA alone | IDA 9.1 `ubuntu-libc6-dev` | IDA 9.1 `debian-libc6-dev` | This project, libc | This project, libc + libgcc |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| x86-64 (1,059) | 0 | 71.6% · 93.3% | 0.5% · 31.3% | 80.8% · 99.9% | **85.2% · 99.9%** |
| i386 (993) | 0 | 18.1% · 87.4% | 64.8% · 90.7% | 78.9% · 99.9% | **86.4% · 99.9%** |
| AArch64 (911) | 0 | 39.2% · 89.5% | 25.8% · 76.3% | 55.9% · 98.6% | **65.4% · 98.8%** |

<sub>Each cell is coverage · precision. IDA applies no libc signature to these binaries by itself.
Its own signatures merge several releases, so the one to pick depends on the distribution
(Ubuntu's is far better on x86-64, Debian's on i386), and their errors are mostly look-alike
stubs, `.cold` fragments and code from other releases. The denominators include functions no
libc signature can name (`main`, the program's own code, CRT start-up). These programs were
built against glibc 2.39, the release our signatures were picked for; IDA's cover several.
Raw results: `devtools/eval_flirt.py`.</sub>

### Runtime identification on CTF binaries

All 19 ELF and PE files of the 强网杯 (QWB) 2023–2025 reverse-engineering challenges, 63.6 MB:

| Class | Files | Result |
|---|--:|---|
| Static x86-64 glibc | 5 | glibc 2.31, 2.35 (×3), 2.39; two of them with tampered ELF headers. All four that carry a GCC/Ubuntu toolchain string agree with it. |
| Dynamic ELF, incl. a 41 MB shared object | 7 | Classified dynamic, with a `GLIBC_2.x` lower bound |
| Windows PE | 6 | MSVC (Rich header), MinGW and .NET recognised |
| Static MIPS | 1 | Reported as unsupported, no verdict |

None was misclassified. The command line identifies all 19 in 2.0 s.

### Flowcharts on real challenges

For seven QWB challenges, the 40 largest functions of each (238 functions) were charted and
every variable of each (up to 40, 4,500 in all) was followed: no error, no two shapes
overlapping, no line through a shape. The largest chart has 2,089 lines (statements and
conditions); no chart took more than 1.0 s to make, nor following another variable more than
1.0 s. The test suite lays out 150 random function shapes with the same checks. Opening calls
was exercised three calls deep in xrtFuze and in ez_vm (QWB 2024, a VM interpreter); a called
function's chart took 11–41 ms to make.

### Undo

Every change the plugin writes (names, types, library flags, comments) goes through a journal.
On 36 headless configurations (the files above, test programs in both UI languages, the
ground-truth binaries), **Undo** left behind nothing the plugin had written; the only
differences were types Hex-Rays itself inferred while decompiling. The data-flow and flowchart
windows write nothing.

## How it works

**Fingerprints.** For each of 29 builds per architecture (glibc 2.19–2.44, musl 1.1.24 and
1.2.2–1.2.6, from Ubuntu and Alpine packages), every function's leading FLAIR pattern gives its
longest wildcard-free run of 10–24 bytes. The 12–14 k anchors per architecture are indexed by
their rarest 8-byte window, so one pass over the executable bytes does one dictionary probe per
offset. A build's score is the fraction of its anchors present; confidence comes from the margin
over the runner-up and is cross-checked against a toolchain string in `.comment`. Static versus
dynamic comes from the program headers, and a tampered `EI_CLASS`/`EI_DATA` is decoded in
whichever layout gives a valid machine.

**Signatures that match linked code.** `pelf` wildcards what a relocation fills in, judged from
the object file, but static linking rewrites more:

| Arch | In `libc.a` | In the linked binary | Bytes `pelf` leaves literal |
|---|---|---|---|
| x86-64 | `mov rcx, [rip+__libc_errno@gottpoff]` &nbsp;`48 8B 0D` | `mov rcx, -0x40` &nbsp;`48 C7 C1` | REX, opcode, ModRM |
| AArch64 | `adrp x0, sym` &nbsp;pattern `......90` | `600400F0` | top byte (`immlo`) |
| AArch64 | `ldr x0, [x0, #:lo12:sym]` &nbsp;pattern `....40F9` | `002446F9` | high bits of `imm12` |
| i386 | `mov eax, [ebx+sym@GOT]` &nbsp;`8B 83` | `mov eax, imm32` &nbsp;`C7 C0` | opcode, ModRM |

[`tools/flair_relax.py`](tools/flair_relax.py) wildcards those bytes before `sigmake` runs;
patterns with fewer than 10 fixed bytes and byte-identical modules are left out rather than
guessed. The GCC runtime (`libgcc`, `libgcc_eh`, `crtbeginT.o`) is signed separately for GCC 4.8
to 15 and applied with the libc signature.

**Data flow.** The Hex-Rays ctree is turned into a neutral statement tree. Each statement is a
site with what it reads and writes; reaching definitions run as bit sets over the control flow;
points-to is computed per site, so `p = buf; *p = x` writes `buf`'s memory. Calls are modelled by
what they do with their arguments: `fread` only writes its buffer, `memcpy` copies, an unknown
call may write what it is handed. A slice goes backward to the sources and forward to the uses.

**Flowchart.** The statement tree becomes a structured layout (sequence, if, loops, switch,
break/continue, goto), placed by contours: the main line straight down, a branch that ends
(an error that returns) in a column beside its condition, loops closed on the left. Lines are
routed orthogonally around the shapes. A called chart hangs in a panel under its call, scaled
to its width; how far a call is zoomed into decides when its chart is made, faded in and
dropped.

## Install

1. Download `ida_better_reverse.zip` and `ibr-sigs.zip` from the
   [latest release](https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/latest).
2. Unzip both into your IDA user directory (`%APPDATA%\Hex-Rays\IDA Pro\` on Windows,
   `~/.idapro/` on Linux and macOS). This adds `plugins/ida_better_reverse.py`, the code in
   `plugins/ida_better_reverse/`, and 117 signatures under `sig/pc/` and `sig/arm/`.
3. Restart IDA.

IDA Pro 7.4–9.x with Python 3 (developed and tested on 9.1). The windows use PyQt5 (IDA 9.1 and earlier) or PySide6 (9.2 and
later). Annotations, data flow and flowcharts need the Hex-Rays decompiler; detection and FLIRT
do not. The plugin opens no network connections. Upgrading from 2.3 or earlier: the new entry
file replaces the old single `ida_better_reverse.py`.

## Usage

| Where | Key | |
|---|---|---|
| Anywhere | **Ctrl-Alt-G** | The panel: **Detect**, **Run** (signatures, prototypes, notes), **Undo** |
| Pseudocode | **Ctrl-Alt-F** or right-click | Data flow of the variable under the cursor |
| Data flow window | **Space** | Text ⇄ flowchart, in the same place |
| Both | double-click · Esc · Ctrl-Enter · mouse side buttons | Follow a variable · back · forward |
| Flowchart | wheel · drag | Zoom about the mouse (into calls) · move |
| Flowchart | click · Esc | The path to a step · back to the variable |
| Flowchart | double-click a function · Enter | Go to it · jump to the line in the pseudocode |

A local variable double-clicked in the pseudocode is followed by an open data-flow window too.
The UI and the notes are in English and Chinese.

Without IDA (from the unzipped `plugins/` folder or a clone):

```console
$ python ida_better_reverse.py ./target                  # ranked candidates and verdict
$ python ida_better_reverse.py ./target --top 8 --json   # machine-readable
$ python ida_better_reverse.py ./target --repair out     # fix tampered EI_CLASS/EI_DATA so IDA can load it
```

## Building from source

The source is `src/ibr`: `core/` (detection, decoders, layout; no IDA imports), `analysis/`
(data flow, flowchart), `app/` (use cases against `ports.py`), `adapters/` (idaapi, Hex-Rays,
Qt, command line). `python tools/package.py` builds `dist/ida_better_reverse.zip`;
`python -m pytest tests` runs without IDA. The fingerprints and signatures are rebuilt from
public packages with Hex-Rays FLAIR: `tools/build-db.py`, `build-fingerprint.py`,
`make-sigs.py`.

## Limitations

- Static identification needs the build in the database; libraries built by other
  distributions with other flags match less well. Dynamic binaries get a lower bound.
- Runtime identification and signatures cover x86-64, i386 and AArch64. On AArch64 a function
  inside a multi-function object module is named only when the module's first function matches.
- Library functions byte-identical to another are left unnamed.
- Data flow is per function: values passed through globals between functions, or through
  pointers stored in memory by other functions, are not followed across calls.
- An opened call's panel covers the part of the calling chart below it until you zoom out.

## License

The code is [MIT](LICENSE). The fingerprints and signatures are generated with Hex-Rays FLAIR
from public Ubuntu and Alpine packages:

| Library | Packages | License |
|---|---|---|
| glibc | `libc6-dev`, `libc6-dev-i386` | LGPL-2.1-or-later |
| musl | `musl-dev` | MIT |
| GCC runtime | `libgcc-N-dev`, `lib32gcc-N-dev` | GPL-3.0 with the GCC Runtime Library Exception |

They hold function names, checksums and short byte patterns: the first 32 bytes of each function
with relocated bytes wildcarded, plus a few distinguishing bytes where two functions would
otherwise collide, and one 10–24-byte run per function in the fingerprint. No library or
complete function is redistributed; FLAIR is not included. Inspired by the community FLIRT
collection [`push0ebp/sig-database`](https://github.com/push0ebp/sig-database), whose data is
not used here.

```

`README.zh-CN.md`:

```md
<div align="center">

# IDA Better Reverse

**认出去符号静态程序里的 libc 并命名，在伪代码里写明每个调用的含义，再把任意函数画成一张可以对着调用一层层放大进去的流程图。**

[![Release](https://img.shields.io/github/v/release/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse?color=4c1)](https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/latest)
[![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-blue)](LICENSE)
![IDA Pro 7.4–9.x](https://img.shields.io/badge/IDA%20Pro-7.4%E2%80%939.x-555)
![x86-64 · i386 · AArch64](https://img.shields.io/badge/arch-x86--64%20%C2%B7%20i386%20%C2%B7%20AArch64-555)

[English](README.md) · 简体中文

</div>

<picture>
  <source srcset="docs/demo.zh-CN.avif" type="image/avif">
  <img src="docs/demo-poster.zh-CN.png" alt="IDA Better Reverse 识别静态程序中的 glibc 2.39、恢复函数名并注释伪代码" width="100%">
</picture>
<p align="center"><sub>IDA Pro 9.1，去符号的静态 x86-64 程序，一键完成（v2.2.0 录制）。<a href="https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/download/v2.2.0/demo.zh-CN.mp4">MP4，1080p60</a> · 数据流与流程图见<a href="#数据流与流程图">单独的视频</a>。</sub></p>

IDA Better Reverse 是一个 IDA Pro 插件，面向 CTF 逆向、pwn 题和不少 Linux 恶意样本最常见的形态：去符号、静态链接。它判断程序里链进来的是哪个 C 运行库构建（glibc 或 musl，精确到发行版本），用为这个构建以及同时链入的 GCC 运行时专门生成的 FLIRT 签名给库函数命名，并在 Hex-Rays 输出里写明每个 libc 调用和系统调用在做什么。之后它帮你读程序自己的代码：单独看一个变量的数据流，或者把整个函数画成教科书式的流程图，对着某个调用放大，它就在原地展开成被调函数的流程图。插件是纯 Python，数据内置；入口文件不装 IDA 也能当命令行工具用。

## 命名与注释

### 从字节认出 libc 并命名

去符号的静态程序里，源码中的 `printf` 只剩一个 `sub_4776D0`。**检测**用各 glibc / musl 构建的函数字节指纹在程序里的命中比例给候选排序；**执行**套用为胜出构建及其 GCC 运行时生成的 FLIRT 签名，并给命名后的函数加上原型。在 Butterfly 上：glibc 2.39，一键命名 838 个函数，套用 143 个原型。

<p align="center"><img src="docs/img/detect.zh-CN.png" alt="检测结果：glibc 2.39，置信度高，静态 x86-64，与 2.40–2.43 的排名" width="46%"></p>

### 写明每个调用的含义

反编译器钩子在每个调用旁边写出它对实际传入的值做了什么：`open` 标志、`mmap` 权限（RWX 会标出）、信号、`ptrace` 请求、套接字类型、printf / scanf 每个格式符对应的参数、按架构命名的 `syscall(n)`。成串的 MMX / SSE / AVX 内建函数折叠成一个普通算式，并说明它和标量运算不同的地方（按通道、无进位）。注释在函数显示时绘制，不写入数据库。

<p align="center"><img src="docs/img/notes.zh-CN.png" alt="Butterfly 的加密循环：_m_pxor/_m_por/_m_paddb 注释成 *v17 ^ v24[0]、rotl(v18, 8)（交换每个 16 位通道的两个字节）、rotl(v19, 1) + v24[0]（逐字节相加，字节间不进位）；printf 的参数逐个对应" width="100%"></p>

## 数据流与流程图

<picture>
  <source srcset="docs/flow.zh-CN.avif" type="image/avif">
  <img src="docs/flow-poster.zh-CN.png" alt="密钥的数据流、整个函数的流程图，再对着 xrtFuze 的调用连续放大三层" width="100%">
</picture>
<p align="center"><sub>IDA Pro 9.1 实录：先是 Butterfly（强网杯 2025），再在 xrtFuze（强网杯 2023）里连续放大三层。<a href="https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/download/v2.4.0/flow.zh-CN.mp4">MP4，1080p60</a></sub></p>

### 单独看一个变量

在变量（或全局变量）上按 **Ctrl-Alt-F**，打开一个窗口，只留下它的值从哪里来（▲）、读写它的行（●）、它流向哪里（▼），以及包住这些行的 if / 循环头，其余全部隐藏。它能跟着值穿过内存：基于控制流的到达定义、按对象的指向分析、指针拷贝，以及通过参数写内存的调用（`fread`、`memcpy`、未知的 `encrypt(buf, out)`）。双击另一个变量改为跟踪它，Esc 返回。

<p align="center"><img src="docs/img/dataflow.zh-CN.png" alt="main 中 v23 的数据流：密钥载入、拷进 v24、被 MMX 循环使用、传给 sub_401CA0" width="80%"></p>

### 把函数画成流程图

按 **空格**，窗口变成整个函数的流程图，画法和教科书一致：语句按顺序排列，条件是菱形、出口标是 / 否，输入输出是平行四边形，调用程序自己的函数用双竖边框，循环在左侧回跳、右侧退出，出错返回的分支单独放一列。运算写成算式（`v19 <<< 1`、`(每字节)`），不是内建函数名。当前变量标黄；它的来源标紫（▲），由它算出来的标蓝（▼），都只标在真正参与计算的那几行：来源在它所在行和上方，去向在下方。与它无关的行淡化，左上角有图例。单击某一步，亮出走到这一步的路径。

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/img/flowchart.zh-CN.png" alt="Butterfly main 的流程图，当前变量为密钥 v23：v24、v18、v19 和缓冲区指针标为由它算出" width="49%">
  <img src="docs/img/path.zh-CN.png" alt="单击成功输出的那一步：只亮通往它的条件和出口" width="49%">
</p>

### 对着调用放大，原地展开

程序自己的函数调用旁都有一个 ⊕。鼠标停在上面，会先弹出被调函数流程图的缩略图。对着调用放大，它的流程图就挂在调用下方展开；继续放大，里面的调用也能这样一层层打开，想钻多深都行。图例第一行的面包屑显示当前在哪一层。如果当前跟踪的值作为实参传了进去，被调函数里接收它的参数会接着点亮。放大到之前不反编译任何东西，每次事件循环只处理一个函数，缩小后释放。

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/img/preview.zh-CN.png" alt="鼠标停在 sub_804ADE9 上：显示它的流程图缩略图，提示滚轮放大展开" width="100%">
</p>
<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/img/nested.zh-CN.png" alt="xrtFuze 第二层：v8 = sub_804AB46(*ptr) 在调用下方展开" width="49%">
  <img src="docs/img/inside.zh-CN.png" alt="第三层：main › sub_804ADE9 › sub_804ACDF › sub_804AB46，即 flag 校验" width="49%">

## 评测

所有数字均用 IDA Pro 9.1（idalib 与图形界面）在 AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395 上实测。

### 函数命名与真值对比

两个小 C 程序用 Ubuntu 24.04 的 GCC 13.3、glibc 2.39 以 `-static` 编译出 x86-64、i386、AArch64 三个二进制，再去符号。签名套用到去符号版本，重复套用直到某一轮不再新增命名；每个名字都和未去符号版本同一地址上的全部符号比对（`read`/`__read`/`__libc_read` 这类别名算对）。覆盖率 = 命名正确数 / 未去符号版本符号表中的全部函数，精确率 = 命名正确数 / 命名总数。

| 二进制（函数数） | 仅 IDA | IDA 9.1 `ubuntu-libc6-dev` | IDA 9.1 `debian-libc6-dev` | 本项目，libc | 本项目，libc + libgcc |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| x86-64（1,059） | 0 | 71.6% · 93.3% | 0.5% · 31.3% | 80.8% · 99.9% | **85.2% · 99.9%** |
| i386（993） | 0 | 18.1% · 87.4% | 64.8% · 90.7% | 78.9% · 99.9% | **86.4% · 99.9%** |
| AArch64（911） | 0 | 39.2% · 89.5% | 25.8% · 76.3% | 55.9% · 98.6% | **65.4% · 98.8%** |

<sub>每格为覆盖率 · 精确率。这些程序上 IDA 自己不会套用任何 libc 签名。IDA 自带签名把多个版本合在一起，该选哪个取决于发行版（x86-64 上 Ubuntu 的好得多，i386 上 Debian 的好），错误主要是长得像的短函数、`.cold` 片段和其他版本的代码。分母包括任何 libc 签名都无法命名的函数（`main`、程序自身代码、CRT 启动代码）。测试程序用的是 glibc 2.39，正是本项目签名所选的版本，IDA 的签名则覆盖多个版本。原始结果：`devtools/eval_flirt.py`。</sub>

### CTF 程序的运行库识别

强网杯 2023–2025 逆向题全部 19 个 ELF 与 PE 文件，共 63.6 MB：

| 类别 | 文件数 | 结果 |
|---|--:|---|
| 静态 x86-64 glibc | 5 | glibc 2.31、2.35（×3）、2.39，其中两个 ELF 头被篡改。四个带 GCC / Ubuntu 工具链字符串的，结果与之一致。 |
| 动态 ELF，含一个 41 MB 的共享库 | 7 | 判为动态，给出 `GLIBC_2.x` 下限 |
| Windows PE | 6 | 识别出 MSVC（Rich 头）、MinGW 与 .NET |
| 静态 MIPS | 1 | 报告不支持，不下结论 |

无一误判。命令行识别全部 19 个文件共用 2.0 秒。

### 真实题目上的流程图

对 7 道强网杯题各取最大的 40 个函数（共 238 个）画流程图，并对每个函数的每个变量（每函数最多 40 个，共 4,500 个）做跟踪：没有报错，没有两个图形重叠，没有连线穿过图形。最大的一张图有 2,089 行（语句和条件）；画图最长 1.0 秒，换跟踪变量最长 1.0 秒。测试集另用 150 个随机函数结构做同样的检查。放大展开在 xrtFuze 和 ez_vm（强网杯 2024，一个虚拟机解释器）上都测到了三层；生成一个被调函数的流程图用时 11–41 毫秒。

### 撤回

插件写入的所有改动（名称、类型、库函数标记、注释）都记入日志。在 36 个无界面配置（上面的文件、两种界面语言下的测试程序、真值二进制）上，**撤回**后插件写过的东西一样不剩；唯一的差异是 Hex-Rays 反编译时自己推断的类型。数据流和流程图窗口不写数据库。

## 原理

**指纹。** 每个架构 29 个构建（glibc 2.19–2.44、musl 1.1.24 与 1.2.2–1.2.6，取自 Ubuntu 和 Alpine 软件包），每个函数开头的 FLAIR 模式取一段 10–24 字节、不含通配的最长片段作为锚点。每个架构 1.2–1.4 万个锚点按各自最稀有的 8 字节窗口建索引，扫描可执行字节一遍，每个偏移只查一次字典。构建的得分是其锚点在程序中出现的比例；置信度看与第二名的差距，并与 `.comment` 里的工具链字符串交叉核对。静态 / 动态由程序头判断；被篡改的 `EI_CLASS` / `EI_DATA` 按能解出有效机器类型的布局解码。

**适配链接后代码的签名。** `pelf` 只把目标文件里重定位要填的字节通配，但静态链接改动得更多：

| 架构 | `libc.a` 中 | 链接后 | `pelf` 未通配的字节 |
|---|---|---|---|
| x86-64 | `mov rcx, [rip+__libc_errno@gottpoff]` &nbsp;`48 8B 0D` | `mov rcx, -0x40` &nbsp;`48 C7 C1` | REX、操作码、ModRM |
| AArch64 | `adrp x0, sym` &nbsp;模式 `......90` | `600400F0` | 最高字节（`immlo`） |
| AArch64 | `ldr x0, [x0, #:lo12:sym]` &nbsp;模式 `....40F9` | `002446F9` | `imm12` 高位 |
| i386 | `mov eax, [ebx+sym@GOT]` &nbsp;`8B 83` | `mov eax, imm32` &nbsp;`C7 C0` | 操作码、ModRM |

[`tools/flair_relax.py`](tools/flair_relax.py) 在 `sigmake` 之前把这些字节通配；固定字节不足 10 个的模式和字节完全相同的模块宁可不命名也不猜。GCC 运行时（`libgcc`、`libgcc_eh`、`crtbeginT.o`）为 GCC 4.8 到 15 单独生成签名，与 libc 签名一同套用。

**数据流。** Hex-Rays 的 ctree 先转成中立的语句树。每条语句是一个站点，记录它读写什么；到达定义以位集在控制流上计算；指向关系按站点计算，所以 `p = buf; *p = x` 写的是 `buf` 的内存。调用按它对参数做什么建模：`fread` 只写缓冲区，`memcpy` 是拷贝，未知调用可能写它拿到的内存。切片向后找来源，向前找去向。

**流程图。** 语句树转成结构化布局（顺序、if、循环、switch、break / continue、goto），按轮廓摆放：主线一路向下，以返回结束的分支（出错返回）放在条件旁边单独一列，循环在左侧闭合。连线绕开图形正交布线。被调函数的流程图挂在调用下方的面板里，按面板宽度缩放；调用被放大的程度决定它何时生成、淡入和释放。

## 安装

1. 从[最新发布页](https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse/releases/latest)下载 `ida_better_reverse.zip` 和 `ibr-sigs.zip`。
2. 两个都解压到 IDA 用户目录（Windows 为 `%APPDATA%\Hex-Rays\IDA Pro\`，Linux / macOS 为 `~/.idapro/`）。会放入 `plugins/ida_better_reverse.py`、代码目录 `plugins/ida_better_reverse/`，以及 `sig/pc/`、`sig/arm/` 下的 117 个签名。
3. 重启 IDA。

需要 IDA Pro 7.4–9.x 与 Python 3（在 9.1 上开发和测试）。窗口使用 PyQt5（IDA 9.1 及以前）或 PySide6（9.2 及以后）。注释、数据流和流程图需要 Hex-Rays 反编译器；检测和 FLIRT 不需要。插件不发起任何网络连接。从 2.3 及以前版本升级：新的入口文件会替换旧的单文件 `ida_better_reverse.py`。

## 用法

| 位置 | 按键 | 作用 |
|---|---|---|
| 任意处 | **Ctrl-Alt-G** | 打开面板：**检测**、**执行**（签名、原型、注释）、**撤回** |
| 伪代码 | **Ctrl-Alt-F** 或右键 | 光标处变量的数据流 |
| 数据流窗口 | **空格** | 文字 ⇄ 流程图，原地切换 |
| 两种窗口 | 双击 · Esc · Ctrl-Enter · 鼠标侧键 | 跟踪变量 · 后退 · 前进 |
| 流程图 | 滚轮 · 拖动 | 以鼠标为中心缩放（可放大进调用）· 平移 |
| 流程图 | 单击 · Esc | 走到这一步的路径 · 回到变量高亮 |
| 流程图 | 双击函数名 · 回车 | 跳到该函数 · 跳到伪代码中的这一行 |

数据流窗口开着时，在伪代码里双击局部变量，窗口也会跟着切换。界面与注释支持中英文。

不装 IDA（在解压后的 `plugins/` 目录或仓库里）：

```console
$ python ida_better_reverse.py ./target                  # 候选排名与结论
$ python ida_better_reverse.py ./target --top 8 --json   # 机器可读输出
$ python ida_better_reverse.py ./target --repair out     # 修复被篡改的 EI_CLASS/EI_DATA，让 IDA 能载入
```

## 从源码构建

源码在 `src/ibr`：`core/`（检测、解码、布局，不引用 IDA）、`analysis/`（数据流、流程图）、`app/`（面向 `ports.py` 的用例）、`adapters/`（idaapi、Hex-Rays、Qt、命令行）。`python tools/package.py` 生成 `dist/ida_better_reverse.zip`；`python -m pytest tests` 不需要 IDA。指纹和签名用 Hex-Rays FLAIR 从公开软件包重建：`tools/build-db.py`、`build-fingerprint.py`、`make-sigs.py`。

## 局限

- 静态识别要求该构建在数据库中；其他发行版用不同参数编译的库匹配度会下降。动态程序只给出下限。
- 运行库识别和签名覆盖 x86-64、i386、AArch64。AArch64 上，多函数目标模块里的后续函数只有在模块首个函数匹配时才会命名。
- 与其他函数字节完全相同的库函数不命名。
- 数据流以函数为单位：经由全局变量、或由其他函数存进内存的指针在函数之间传递的值，不跨调用跟踪。
- 展开的调用面板会盖住调用下方的那部分流程图，缩小即恢复。

## 许可

代码采用 [MIT](LICENSE)。指纹与签名用 Hex-Rays FLAIR 从公开的 Ubuntu、Alpine 软件包生成：

| 库 | 软件包 | 许可 |
|---|---|---|
| glibc | `libc6-dev`、`libc6-dev-i386` | LGPL-2.1-or-later |
| musl | `musl-dev` | MIT |
| GCC 运行时 | `libgcc-N-dev`、`lib32gcc-N-dev` | GPL-3.0，附 GCC 运行库例外条款 |

其中只有函数名、校验和与短字节模式：每个函数开头 32 字节（重定位字节已通配），两函数冲突时附加少量区分字节；指纹中每个函数一段 10–24 字节。不再分发任何库或完整函数；不包含 FLAIR。灵感来自社区 FLIRT 合集 [`push0ebp/sig-database`](https://github.com/push0ebp/sig-database)，但未使用其数据。

```

`docs/adr/0001-challenge-map.md`:

```md
# ADR-0001：解题地图（Challenge Map）

- 状态：提议（Proposed），待维护者确认
- 日期：2026-10-13
- 适用版本：v2.4 起（在 v2.3.0 的 ports / adapters 结构上实现）
- 代码位置：`src/ibr/analysis/triage.py`（目前是空模块）

## 1. 背景

新手打开一道逆向题，第一个问题通常是"从哪里看起"。本插件已经能给静态程序的 libc 函数命名、在伪代码里注释调用，但还不能回答这个问题。

解题地图的目标：插件跑完之后，给出一份可点击的清单，回答这几件事：

- 程序从哪里开始执行，包括 main 之前就会运行的代码；
- 输入从哪里进来；
- 在哪里做判定，判定时拿什么数据比较；
- 成功和失败分别走到哪里；
- 有没有会改变程序行为的反分析代码。

做方案之前做了两项调研：

1. **题目实测**：用强网杯 2023–2025 的 21 道逆向题做标准答案。其中 15 道是 IDA 能分析的原生程序，6 道是 .NET、字节码、APK 等。
2. **竞品调研**：调查了 27 个相关工具，每条结论都有来源。

原始材料（不随仓库发布）：

- 标准答案：`reverse-skill/work/ibr-challenge-map-research/evidence/gt/*.json|md`
- 探针输出：`evidence/probe/`
- 汇总：`notes/aggregate.md`
- 竞品报告：`notes/competitors.md`

## 2. 调研结论

### 2.1 题目形态：经典 crackme 是少数

15 道原生题按形态分：

| 形态 | 数量 | 题目 |
|---|--:|---|
| 输入 → 比较 → 对/错（经典 crackme） | 3 | Vanguard-ezre, 2023 ezre, Vanguard-babyre |
| 自定义虚拟机 / JIT | 3 | xrtFuze, ez_vm, remem |
| 协议 + 流量包 | 2 | ZZtransfer, iked |
| 模拟器 | 2 | mips（emu + mips_bin） |
| 迷宫 / 游戏 | 1 | boxx |
| 父进程调试子进程（nanomite） | 1 | tradre |
| 只加密不校验（编码器） | 2 | Butterfly, fancy |
| 自修改代码 | 1 | snake |

27 条判定记录里：

| 期望数据的形式 | 条数 |
|---|--:|
| 全局数组 | 11 |
| 根本没有比较 | 7 |
| 栈上立即数拼出来 | 3 |
| 来自外部文件 | 3 |
| 运行时计算 | 3 |

**结论：** 只按"找 memcmp / 找 Correct 字符串"设计，会在大多数题上什么也给不出。地图必须先判断题目形态，再按形态给条目。

### 2.2 一个朴素探针能做到什么

调研时写了一个探针，只用三类信号：按 API 名找调用、按关键词找字符串、找"和全局数据比较"的伪代码行。它在 15 道原生题上的结果：

| 条目 | 找对 |
|---|--:|
| main | 7/15 |
| 输入点 | 7/15 |
| 判定点 | 6/15 |
| **期望数据（密文）** | **0/15** |
| 成功输出 | 6/15 |
| 失败输出 | 7/15 |
| 反分析代码 | 5/11（4 道不适用） |

失败原因按出现频率排列：

1. **main 没找到（8/15）。** 静态程序的 main 没有名字，但有 10 道题都能从 `_start` 里调用 `__libc_start_main` 前装进 rdi 的地址拿到；其余靠 MSVC（`invoke_main`）、MinGW（`__tmainCRTStartup`）和 Rust（`lang_start`）的固定调用链。
2. **main 之前的代码被忽略。** 3 道题的关键代码在 TLS 回调或 `.init_array` 构造函数里。其中 babyre 和 Vanguard-ezre 还会**改写期望数据**，直接导出数组会给出错误的密文。
3. **噪声。** Butterfly 的 100 个关键词字符串里 91 个是 libc 自带文本。boxx、snake 的"反调试"命中全是 C 运行库代码。iked 静态链入的 OpenSSL 没有被 FLIRT 命名，6495 个"用户函数"里混着大量库代码，其中 26 条"反调试"命中都来自 OpenSSL 和 glibc 内部。
4. **头部被篡改的程序直接失效**：ZZtransfer、remem 在 IDA 里是 0 个函数。插件已有 `--repair`，但地图没有利用它。
5. **字符串形态**：短字符串（ez_vm 的 `:)`）被关键词过滤掉；GBK 编码（boxx 的规则说明）没解码；异或后存放的字符串（fancy、mips 模拟器）找不到。
6. **数据流断点**：通过函数指针表调用（Rust）、和栈上立即数比较、代码不在 IDA 函数里（花指令）。
7. **题目附带的文件决定了解法**（9/21）：pcap、VM 程序文件、密文文件、`.abc` 字节码、追加在 PE 尾部的 zip。只看二进制本身不够。

### 2.3 竞品：没有工具做这件事

| 能力 | 现有工具 | 不足 |
|---|---|---|
| 输入 → 判定 → 成功/失败的整体地图 | 无 | 中英文搜索都只找到手工流程（字符串 → 交叉引用 → main） |
| 导出比较用的字节 | findcrypt 只导出已知密码常量；IDA Shift+E 要手选 | 没有工具取 memcmp / 循环比较的操作数 |
| 反分析检测 | capa（Windows 为主）、AntiDebugSeeker（仅 Windows）、DiE（PE） | Linux 静态检测基本空白；都依赖函数名 |
| 交给求解器 | AngryIDA、IDAngr、SENinja、Ponce | 都要手选地址；angr 的自动 libc hook 只对未 strip 的程序有效 |
| 给 AI 用 | ida-pro-mcp、Hex-Rays 官方 MCP（2026-09 公布）、Binary Ninja Sidekick | 是通用工具接口，没有"解题地图"这种高层结论 |

关键事实（来源见竞品报告 §2.1）：capa 在 IDA 里只认有名字的函数，包括 `FUNC_LIB` 或不以 `sub_` 开头的函数。所以在静态程序上，本插件的 FLIRT 命名是 capa 这类工具的**前提**，而不是重复。

## 3. 决策驱动因素

1. **新手可用**：条目要能直接点到地址，文字说"这里在干什么"，不堆原始数据。
2. **准确优先于全面**：错的密文比没有密文更糟（见 babyre）。不确定的条目要标出来。
3. **静态、离线**：不运行题目程序（安全），不联网。
4. **复用已有长处**：libc 命名、调用注释、ELF 修复、ports 结构。
5. **可测**：核心逻辑写在 ports 后面，用这 21 道题做回归。
6. **速度**：中小程序几秒内出结果，大程序有上限，不卡 IDA。
7. **能给机器读**：同一份结果输出成 JSON，供以后的 AI 接口（`adapters/agent.py`）使用。

## 4. 备选方案

| 方案 | 做法 | 优点 | 缺点 | 结论 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. 规则清单 | 按 API 名 + 关键词字符串列条目（即调研探针） | 简单、快 | 实测期望数据 0/15、main 7/15，噪声大，不懂题目形态 | 不采用，作为基线 |
| B. 接入 capa 规则引擎 | 把 capa 当依赖，复用其规则 | Windows 反分析规则现成 | 新增重依赖；没有数据流，取不到比较操作数；Linux 规则空白 | 不作依赖；借鉴其规则格式与"跳过库代码"的经验 |
| C. 符号 / 模拟执行 | 用 angr、Unicorn 求出成功路径 | 能直接出解 | 依赖重、慢；VM、协议题效果差；超出插件定位 | 不做；只导出给求解器的骨架（见 §5.9） |
| D. 交给大模型 | 把反编译结果丢给 LLM 找关键点 | 灵活 | 结果不可复现、难测；数字易错；需要联网或本地模型 | 不作核心；通过 JSON 接口配合 |
| **E. 先分类、再收集证据、再推断角色** | 预检题目形态 → 找入口和 main 之前的代码 → 收集带出处的证据 → 推断每个位置的角色并打分 → 输出 | 覆盖多种形态；每条结论都有证据，可回归测试；纯 Python，可在 ports 后实现 | 规则要随题型扩充；VM、协议题只能给到"关键位置"，给不了解 | **采用** |

## 5. 决策：方案 E

### 5.1 范围

v1 包含：

- **目标预检**：原生 / .NET / APK / 字节码 / 附带文件；头部是否被篡改；是否加壳。
- **入口**：main 以及 main 之前会运行的代码（TLS 回调、`.init_array`、`.ctors`）。
- **输入点**：API 调用、`argv`、读文件。
- **判定点**：库函数比较、循环逐字节比较、长度检查、计数器判定。
- **期望数据**：导出字节，并检查是否有其他代码写它。
- **成功 / 失败出口**：字符串及其分支。
- **反分析标记**：只标出位置和影响，见 §5.4 R7。
- **题目形态提示**：VM 分发器、JIT、编码器、协议、模拟器、启动器。

不做（非目标）：

- 求解、去混淆（控制流平坦化）、VM 指令还原。VM 指令还原另立 ADR，对应 `analysis/vm.py`。
- 运行或调试题目程序。
- 自动修改程序字节。
- .NET、APK、字节码内部的分析：只识别出来，并告诉用户换什么工具。

### 5.2 处理流程

```
预检(target) ──> 入口(entry) ──> 证据收集(evidence) ──> 角色推断(roles) ──> 排序(rank) ──> 输出
   │                │                 │                        │                  │
   │ 形态/附带文件    │ main + 前置代码    │ 调用/字符串/比较/       │ input/check/      │ 去噪、可达性、
   │ 头部篡改→修复    │                 │ 立即数/指令/写者        │ expected/win/fail  │ 置信度
```

1. **预检**：读文件头和同目录文件（只读）。头部被篡改时，提示用 `--repair` 后重新加载，地图本身不改文件。.NET（CLI 头）、APK、Ark `.abc`、EVM 字节码、PE 尾部追加的 zip 只出一条"形态 + 建议工具"。
2. **入口**：
   - 有名字的 main / WinMain 直接用；
   - 静态 ELF 取 `_start` 里调用 `__libc_start_main(_impl)` 前装进 rdi 的地址；
   - MSVC 走 `__scrt_common_main_seh → invoke_main`，MinGW 走 `__tmainCRTStartup`，Rust 取 `lang_start` 前装进 rdi 的地址；
   - 再列出 TLS 目录和 `.init_array` 里属于用户代码的函数，标为"main 之前运行"。
3. **证据收集**：每条证据带地址和出处，类型包括：
   - 调用了哪个已识别函数；
   - 引用了哪个字符串；
   - 伪代码里的比较表达式；
   - 写入全局变量的指令；
   - 可疑指令的密度；
   - 大型 switch 结构。
   证据只从"用户代码"收集：排除 `FUNC_LIB`、导入跳板，以及本插件识别出的 libc / 运行库函数。
4. **角色推断**：用规则把证据组合成角色（规则见 §5.4），每个角色带置信度：确定 / 很可能 / 线索。
5. **排序**：从入口沿调用图计算可达性和距离。不可达的函数降级，但不删除（boxx 的渲染函数就是不可达的死代码，却是关键线索）。
6. **输出**：IDA 里的列表窗口，JSON，以及可选的伪代码注释。

### 5.3 数据模型（core，纯 Python）

```python
MapEntry(
  role,        # entry | pre_main | input | check | expected | success | failure |
               # anti_analysis | shape | companion | note
  ea, func,    # 地址与所在函数（可为空：附带文件、形态提示）
  title,       # 一行说明（中英文，走 i18n）
  detail,      # 补充：比较的长度、数据形式、写者列表……
  confidence,  # "sure" | "likely" | "hint"
  evidence,    # [Evidence(kind, ea, text)]：为什么这么判断
  data,        # 期望数据：{"ea", "len", "bytes", "form", "writers": [...]}
)
```

借鉴（来源见竞品报告 §4）：

- Malcat 检测器的"级别 + 类别"；
- DiE / cracklab 区分"规则确认"和"启发式"；
- findcrypt 列表里的 Hex 列。

### 5.4 v1 规则

| 编号 | 角色 | 信号 | 实测依据（题目） |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | entry | 见 §5.2 第 2 步 | 10 道靠 `__libc_start_main`，3 道靠 CRT 名称，1 道靠 Rust，1 道靠 MSVC 调用链 |
| R2 | pre_main | TLS 目录、`.init_array`、`.ctors` 中的用户函数 | babyre、Vanguard-ezre、ezre |
| R3 | input | 已识别输入函数的调用点（含 `getline`、C++ `istream`）；`argv[n]` 被读取；文件读取后进入用户代码 | ez_vm 用 argv[1]，xrtFuze、Butterfly 读文件 |
| R4 | check | memcmp/strcmp 类调用，取两个操作数；`a[i] != b[i]` 形式的循环比较；与常量比较的长度检查；全局计数器判零 | Vanguard-ezre、babyre、ez_vm、boxx |
| R5 | expected | R4 中非输入一侧的操作数：全局数组按循环上界或长度取字节；栈立即数按写入顺序拼起来；同时列出该数据的所有写者，有写者就标"运行时会被修改" | 11 个全局数组、3 个栈常量；babyre、Vanguard-ezre 有写者 |
| R6 | success / failure | 判定分支两侧的输出调用及其字符串参数；关键词表含中文，短字符串只要紧邻判定就保留；GBK 回退解码 | ez_vm 的 `:)`、boxx 的 GBK 字符串 |
| R7 | anti_analysis | 只标位置和对程序的影响，不给处理办法。在用户代码里出现的：读调试器状态（API 或 PEB 字段）、`/proc/self/status`、`ptrace`、父子进程调试关系、大量 `int3`、时间差判断。若在 main 之前运行或会写入期望数据，单独提示 | 6/15 题有，其中 3 题在 main 之前，2 题会改写期望数据 |
| R8 | shape | 大型 switch + 程序计数器自增 → 虚拟机分发器；mmap/mprotect 可执行 + 间接调用 → JIT / 自修改；读文件 → 循环变换 → 写文件，且没有判定 → 编码器；send/recv + 端口常量 → 协议；嵌入解释器的 API → 启动器 | xrtFuze、ez_vm、remem、snake、Butterfly、fancy、ZZtransfer、iked、ABabyChal |
| R9 | companion | 同目录文件按文件头识别类型；在附带文件里搜索程序中的 4/8 字节常量 | iked 的常量与 pcap 的 SPI 一致；Butterfly 的 encode.dat |

### 5.5 降噪（来自实测）

- **库代码一律不算用户代码**：FLIRT 识别出的函数、libc 断言字符串所在函数，以及调用链只通向库的函数。这一条能去掉 Butterfly 那 91 条 libc 字符串。
- **运行库自带的调用不算反分析**：C 运行库的异常过滤器、malloc 初始化里的取时间、Rust 运行库的信号处理。
- **按调用位置判断**：只报告在用户函数里的调用（借鉴 capa 不报 `IsDebuggerPresent` 的做法）。
- **大程序折叠**：未命名的大块库代码（OpenSSL、Rust std）折叠成一条"静态库代码"。

### 5.6 期望数据的导出

- **格式**与 IDA Shift+E 一致：十六进制、Python `bytes`、C 数组。
- **长度**取循环上界、memcmp 的长度参数或 `strlen` 的比较值；推不出来就只给起始地址，并标为"长度未知"。
- **有写者时不给"干净"的字节**：列出写者（包括 main 之前的代码）并标"运行时会被修改"，避免 babyre 那种拿到错误密文的情况。
- 只读取数据库里的字节，不执行任何代码。

### 5.7 端口变更

`ports.Program` 新增：

- `xrefs_to(ea)` / `xrefs_from(ea)`（区分读、写、调用）；
- `strings()`；
- `entry_points()`；
- `tls_callbacks()`、`init_array()`；
- `segment_of(ea)`；
- `bytes(ea, n)`；
- `imports()`；
- `sibling_files()`：只读，列出题目目录下的文件。

`ports.Decompiler` 新增 `function_tree(ea)`：返回整个函数的中立语法树（语句、if/while/for、赋值、比较、下标、调用），不再只给调用表达式。`analysis/algo.py` 和 `analysis/vm.py` 以后也用这一份接口。

`core/` 和 `app/` 仍不允许 import IDA，由 `tests/test_architecture.py` 检查。

### 5.8 界面

- 面板加一个"解题地图"按钮，打开 IDA 的列表窗口（`ida_kernwin.Choose`）。
- 列：角色 | 地址 | 函数 | 说明 | 置信度。双击跳转。
- 期望数据条目右键可以复制为十六进制、Python 或 C 数组。
- 第一行永远是形态提示，例如"自定义虚拟机：分发器在 sub_8048863"或"这是 .NET 程序，请用 dnSpy"。
- 可选：把每条结论作为注释写到对应地址。复用现有日志，撤回时一并移除。
- 中英文跟随面板语言。

### 5.9 对外输出

- **JSON**：同一份 `MapEntry` 列表加版本号。供命令行 `--map`（通过 idalib 无界面运行）和以后的 AI 接口使用。地址统一写成十六进制字符串，因为大模型容易算错数字（ida-pro-mcp 的文档也提醒了这一点）。
- **求解器骨架（v2，可选）**：
  - 用成功 / 失败地址生成 angr 脚本的 find / avoid；
  - 对插件识别出的 libc 函数地址加 hook，补上 angr 在 strip 过的程序上做不到的部分；
  - 只生成文本，不运行。

### 5.10 性能

- 证据收集以交叉引用为种子：只反编译入口、种子函数，以及从入口出发 2 层以内的函数，上限 60 个函数或 20 秒。
- 超过上限时，列表里写明"已截断"。
- 实测参考：调研探针在 18 道题上反编译最多 40 个函数，大部分 2–10 秒，最大的 emu 约 70 秒（含分析）。

## 6. 评估

- **回归集**：21 道题的标准答案（JSON）放进 `tests/fixtures/challenge_map/`。只放结论和地址，不放题目二进制。在 idalib 上跑完整地图，与标准答案比对。
- **指标**：每个角色的召回率和误报数。期望数据要求字节完全一致，或正确标出"运行时会被修改"。

| 指标（15 道原生题） | 基线 | v1 目标 |
|---|--:|--:|
| main | 7/15 | 15/15 |
| 输入点 | 7/15 | ≥ 12/15 |
| 判定点（有判定的题） | 6/15 | ≥ 10/15 |
| 期望数据 | 0/15 | 可静态导出的题 ≥ 7，有写者的题全部标出 |
| 成功 / 失败出口 | 6/15 / 7/15 | ≥ 11/15 |
| 形态提示 | 无 | VM、编码器、协议、模拟器、非原生题全部给出 |
| 每题误报 | — | 角色为"确定"的条目没有误报 |

- **单元测试**：规则写在 core 里，用 `tests/fakes.py` 构造的语法树测试，不需要 IDA，几秒跑完。
- **整体审计**：插件跑前跑后，伪代码整体（包括变量名）要不变，地图只读。这一条来自 2026-10-13 的教训：当时只比对了注释，没有比对伪代码其余部分。

## 7. 后果

正面：

- 填补了竞品空白：整体地图、比较字节导出、Linux 反分析标记、形态提示。
- 把插件已有的 libc 命名转化成新手能直接用的结论。
- JSON 输出为 AI 接口和求解器骨架打好基础。

负面 / 代价：

- 规则需要维护，新题型要补规则。用回归集控制退化。
- 需要扩充 ports，并新增中立语法树的适配层。工作量主要在 `adapters/ida/ctree.py`。
- VM、协议、模拟器题只能给出关键位置，新手可能期待更多，界面上要写清楚。
- 回归集只有 21 道题，可能过拟合强网杯风格；需要补充其他比赛的题目。

## 8. 分阶段实施

| 阶段 | 内容 | 验收 |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | 预检 + 入口（R1、R2）+ 附带文件（R9）+ 列表窗口 + JSON | main 15/15；非原生题全部给出形态提示 |
| P2 | `function_tree` 端口；R3–R6；降噪；期望数据导出 | 达到 §6 中输入、判定、期望数据、出口的目标 |
| P3 | R7 反分析标记、R8 形态提示 | 6 道有反分析的题全部标出位置；形态提示全对 |
| P4 | 注释落盘与撤回、命令行 `--map`、求解器骨架（可选） | 整体审计伪代码不变；撤回后无残留 |

每个阶段单独发版，README 写明实测结果。

## 9. 未决问题

1. 列表窗口之外是否需要"路径视图"，即入口 → 输入 → 判定 → 出口的有序路径（参考 IDA 9.4 的 Pathfinder）？IDAPython 能否调用 Pathfinder 尚未核实。
2. 读同目录文件属于"读取用户数据"，是否默认开启？建议默认开启但只读，在面板上可以关闭。
3. 回归集里的标准答案能否公开？只含地址和结论，不含题目文件。需要确认各比赛的再分发要求。
4. 是否吸收 capa 规则格式（YAML），让用户自己扩充 R7、R8？v1 先用 Python 规则，等规则稳定后再定。

```

`ida_better_reverse.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""
IDA Better Reverse — know your binary before you read a single line.

An IDA Pro plugin (and standalone CLI) that enriches a disassembly with the context you
normally have to dig for by hand:

  1. Runtime/compiler detection — identifies the C runtime or compiler of a target,
     even when it is stripped and statically linked, using function byte-fingerprint
     matching (glibc 2.19-2.44, musl 1.1.24-1.2.6) across x86_64 / i386 / aarch64,
     GLIBC_2.x version requirements, the .comment toolchain string, Go/Rust build
     strings, PE Rich headers, and UPX / PyInstaller / .NET packers.
  2. FLIRT auto-apply — applies the matching glibc/musl FLIRT signature so library
     functions get their real names back.
  3. libc annotations — adds a one-line "what it does" note to recognised libc
     functions (fopen -> open a file, returns FILE*, ...).
  4. Decompiler annotations — in the pseudocode, annotates library calls and MMX/SSE
     intrinsics with their real arguments AND decoded constants. These render live via
     a Hex-Rays hook (always fresh, nothing written), or can be persisted into the
     database as reversible comments.

  - In IDA:   unzip the release into your IDA user directory, so that `plugins/` holds this
              file and the `ida_better_reverse/` folder, then press Ctrl-Alt-G.
  - As a CLI: `python ida_better_reverse.py <binary>`.

Your existing names and comments are never clobbered, and every change made in a run
can be reverted with one click (Undo).

This file is only the entry point. The code is the `ibr` package in the folder next to it:
ida_better_reverse/ibr when installed, src/ibr in a source checkout.

Repo:    https://github.com/FPSZ/ida-better-reverse
License: MIT
"""
import os as _os
import sys as _sys


def _package_root():
    here = _os.path.dirname(_os.path.abspath(__file__))
    for root in (_os.path.join(here, "ida_better_reverse"), _os.path.join(here, "src")):
        if _os.path.isfile(_os.path.join(root, "ibr", "__init__.py")):
            return root
    raise ImportError(
        "IDA Better Reverse: the folder ida_better_reverse/ is missing next to %s. "
        "Unzip the whole release archive into the IDA user directory. "
        "/ 缺少与本文件同目录的 ida_better_reverse/ 文件夹，请把发布的压缩包完整解压到 IDA 用户目录。" % here)


def _use(root):
    # a single-file build (2.3 and earlier) serves `ibr` from an import hook placed before
    # the path finder; while it is there, `import ibr` would load that old copy instead
    for f in [f for f in _sys.meta_path if getattr(f, "_ibr_bundle", False)]:
        _sys.meta_path.remove(f)
        old = getattr(f, "base", "?")
        print("[IDA Better Reverse] an older single-file copy is also installed, delete it: %s\n"
              "[IDA Better Reverse] 还装着旧版单文件插件，请删除：%s" % (old, old))
    # a reload of this file, or another copy of the plugin, replaces the loaded package
    for m in [m for m in _sys.modules if m == "ibr" or m.startswith("ibr.")]:
        del _sys.modules[m]
    while root in _sys.path:
        _sys.path.remove(root)
    _sys.path.insert(0, root)


_use(_package_root())

# ======================================================================
#  Public names (kept from the single-file versions up to 2.3)
# ======================================================================
from ibr import __version__                             # noqa: E402,F401
from ibr.core.detect import detect                       # noqa: E402,F401
from ibr.core.names import canon, resolve_variant        # noqa: E402,F401
from ibr.core.i18n import set_lang                      # noqa: E402,F401

try:
    import idaapi                                         # noqa: F401
    _IN_IDA = True
except Exception:
    _IN_IDA = False

if _IN_IDA:
    from ibr.adapters.ida.service import *               # noqa: E402,F401,F403
    from ibr.adapters.ida.panel import open_panel        # noqa: E402,F401
    from ibr.adapters.ida.plugin import IBIPlugin, PLUGIN_ENTRY   # noqa: E402,F401


def __getattr__(name):
    """State that now lives in the package, readable under its single-file name."""
    if name == "LANG":
        from ibr.core import i18n
        return i18n.LANG
    if name == "_panel" and _IN_IDA:
        from ibr.adapters.ida import panel
        return panel._panel
    raise AttributeError(name)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    from ibr.adapters.cli import main
    main()

`src/ibr/__init__.py`:

```py
"""IDA Better Reverse — know your binary before you read a single line.

Layout (ports and adapters):
  core/      pure Python, no IDA imports: detection, decoders, note text, undo journal
  ports.py   what the application needs from a disassembler (database, decompiler, signatures)
  app/       use cases (detect, apply signatures, annotate, undo) written against the ports
  adapters/  IDA (idaapi, Hex-Rays, Qt panel) and the standalone CLI
  analysis/  analyses of the program's own code: data flow, flowchart (more planned)

The release is a zip built by tools/package.py: the entry file ida_better_reverse.py
plus this package, installed as plugins/ida_better_reverse.py and plugins/ida_better_reverse/ibr.
"""
__version__ = "2.4.0"

`src/ibr/adapters/agent.py`:

```py
"""Interface for AI agents (not implemented yet).

Planned: an MCP server and a JSON command line that drive the same use cases as the
panel (ibr.app), so a model can run the plugin and read its results. It is meant to work
next to an IDA MCP server: that one reads and edits the database at a low level
(decompile, rename, xrefs); this one answers higher-level questions.

Design goals, so that small models can use it too:
  - few tools, each answering one question: overview (runtime, arch, packer, tampering,
    entry/main), interesting functions (ranked, one line each), notes for one function
    (pseudocode with the call-site notes inline), apply (signatures + comments, undoable),
    undo; later the challenge map, algorithm and VM analyses from ibr.analysis
  - compact JSON with a fixed schema, addresses as hex strings, bounded lengths
  - two modes: inside a running IDA (shares the database with the IDA MCP server) and
    headless through idalib (`python ida_better_reverse.py <binary> --agent ...`)
"""

```

`src/ibr/adapters/cli.py`:

```py
"""Standalone command line: detect a binary's runtime, or repair its ELF ident."""
import argparse
import json
import sys

from .. import __version__
from ..core.binfmt import _elf_layout
from ..core.detect import detect
from ..core.fingerprint import _lib_label


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="ida_better_reverse.py",
        description="Detect the C runtime / compiler of a binary (glibc, musl, Go, Rust, MSVC).")
    ap.add_argument("binary", nargs="?", help="target ELF/PE file")
    ap.add_argument("--static", action="store_true", help="force static analysis")
    ap.add_argument("--dynamic", action="store_true", help="force dynamic analysis")
    ap.add_argument("--arch", choices=["x86_64", "i386", "aarch64"], help="override the detected architecture")
    ap.add_argument("--top", type=int, default=5, help="how many candidates to rank (default 5)")
    ap.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="emit the raw result as JSON")
    ap.add_argument("--repair", metavar="OUT",
                    help="write a copy with tampered ELF ident bytes (EI_CLASS/EI_DATA) fixed")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    if not args.binary:
        print("IDA Better Reverse v%s" % __version__)
        ap.print_help(); sys.exit(1)
    try:
        with open(args.binary, "rb") as f:
            raw = f.read()
    except OSError as ex:
        sys.exit("cannot read %s: %s" % (args.binary, ex.strerror or ex))
    if args.repair:
        data = bytearray(raw)
        if data[:4] != b"\x7fELF":
            sys.exit("not an ELF file")
        is64, en, note = _elf_layout(bytes(data))
        if not note:
            sys.exit("ELF ident bytes are consistent; nothing to repair")
        data[4], data[5] = (2 if is64 else 1), (1 if en == "<" else 2)
        try:
            with open(args.repair, "wb") as f:
                f.write(data)
        except OSError as ex:
            sys.exit("cannot write %s: %s" % (args.repair, ex.strerror or ex))
        print("%s.\nwrote %s (bytes 4-5 = %02X %02X)" % (note, args.repair, data[4], data[5]))
        return
    force = "static" if args.static else ("dynamic" if args.dynamic else None)
    r = detect(data=raw, force=force, top=args.top, arch_hint=args.arch)
    if args.json:
        print(json.dumps(r, indent=2)); return
    print("=" * 60)
    print("target    :", args.binary)
    print("type      :", r.get("os"), "/", r.get("kind"), "/", r.get("arch") or "?")
    if r.get("build_id"):
        print("build-id  :", r["build_id"])
    if r.get("arch") and r.get("os") == "windows":
        print("linker    :", r.get("linker"))
    if r.get("ranking"):
        print("-" * 60); print("version ranking (hit-rate  hits/anchors):")
        for v, s, h, n in r["ranking"]:
            print("  %-12s %6.1f%%  %d/%d  %s" % (_lib_label(v), s * 100, h, n, "#" * int(s * 30)))
    print("-" * 60)
    print("verdict   :", r.get("best"), "  confidence =", r.get("confidence"))
    for note in r.get("notes", []):
        print("  -", note)
    print("=" * 60)

`src/ibr/adapters/ida/ctree.py`:

```py
"""Hex-Rays ctree expressions seen through ports.Expr (wrapped lazily, no copy)."""
import ida_hexrays as hr
import ida_lines
import idaapi

from ... import ports

_OPNAME = {hr.cot_call: "call", hr.cot_helper: "helper", hr.cot_num: "num", hr.cot_fnum: "fnum",
           hr.cot_str: "str", hr.cot_obj: "obj", hr.cot_var: "var", hr.cot_ref: "ref",
           hr.cot_cast: "cast"}


class IdaExpr(ports.Expr):
    __slots__ = ("e", "cf")

    def __init__(self, e, cf):
        self.e, self.cf = e, cf

    @property
    def op(self):
        return _OPNAME.get(self.e.op, "other")

    @property
    def x(self):
        return IdaExpr(self.e.x, self.cf) if self.e.x is not None else None

    @property
    def args(self):
        return [IdaExpr(a, self.cf) for a in self.e.a]

    @property
    def value(self):
        return self.e.numval()

    @property
    def size(self):
        return self.e.type.get_size() if self.e.type else 0

    @property
    def is_float(self):
        return bool(self.e.type is not None and self.e.type.is_floating())

    @property
    def string(self):
        return self.e.string

    @property
    def obj_ea(self):
        return self.e.obj_ea

    @property
    def var_name(self):
        return self.cf.lvars[self.e.v.idx].name

    @property
    def helper(self):
        return self.e.helper or ""

    @property
    def ea(self):
        return self.e.ea

    @property
    def index(self):
        return self.e.index

    @property
    def uid(self):
        return self.e.obj_id

    def render(self):
        return ida_lines.tag_remove(self.e.print1(self.cf)).replace("\n", " ")


def calls(cfunc):
    """The function's calls with an address, in pre-order."""
    out = []

    class _V(hr.ctree_visitor_t):
        def __init__(self):
            hr.ctree_visitor_t.__init__(self, hr.CV_FAST)

        def visit_expr(self, e):
            if e.op == hr.cot_call and e.x is not None and e.ea != idaapi.BADADDR:
                out.append(IdaExpr(e, cfunc))
            return 0
    _V().apply_to(cfunc.body, None)
    return out

```

`src/ibr/adapters/ida/flowgraph.py`:

```py
"""The flowchart window: one function drawn as a program flowchart, the way textbooks draw
one. analysis/flowchart.py says what is in it, core/chartlayout.py where it goes; this file
draws it with Qt inside an IDA form.

The lines that belong to the followed variable are drawn in full and its name is marked
wherever it appears. The names it is computed from (▲, on its line and above) and those
computed from it (▼, below) are marked in their own colours on the lines where they take part
in it; everything else is faded, so following another variable only moves the light. A legend in the top-left corner says what the colours mean.
A click on a shape lights the way from the start to it instead (which conditions, which
exit), ending there; a click on empty space or Esc goes back to the variable.
The drawing sits on a canvas, used like a map: drag it anywhere, the wheel zooms about the
mouse, Shift+wheel goes sideways.

Zooming into a call of one of the program's own functions (marked ⊕) opens that function's
chart inside it, and so on further down (core/nest.py says when and where). Resting the mouse
on such a call shows a small picture of that chart first, so it is plain there is more inside. A chart is made
only when its call is zoomed into, one at a time, and dropped when zoomed out again. When the
followed value (or one computed from it, or one it comes from) is handed to the call, the
argument it arrives in is followed inside."""
import html
import math
import re

import ida_kernwin

from ...core import nest
from ...core.chartlayout import route
from ...core.i18n import T
from .qt import QtCore, QtGui, QtWidgets, dark_theme

PAD_X, PAD_Y = 12, 7
BAR = 7                 # the inner sides of a call box
KEEP = 80               # px of the drawing that stay on screen however far it is dragged
GRID = 24               # the canvas's dots, in drawing units
RATIO = 4.0             # a diamond's width to its height
ZOOM = (0.1, 8.0)       # how far the drawing can be zoomed out and in (in, per open chart)
STEP = 1.15             # one notch of the wheel
_KEYWORDS = ("return", "sizeof")
_TOKEN = re.compile(r'"(?:\\.|[^"\\])*"|\b0x[0-9A-Fa-f]+\b|\b\d+\b|\b[A-Za-z_]\w*\b|.')
_IDENT = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z_]\w*")
_STRING = re.compile(r'"(?:\\.|[^"\\])*"')

# light, dark
_COLORS = {
    "bg": ("#FAFAF9", "#1E1F22"),
    "grid": ("#E2E4E8", "#2F3135"),
    "fill": ("#FFFFFF", "#27292D"),
    "line": ("#2F343B", "#C4C8CE"),
    "text": ("#1B1F24", "#DFE1E5"),
    "faded_line": ("#D2D6DC", "#3D4046"),
    "faded_text": ("#A9AFB7", "#5C6067"),
    "keyword": ("#0B57A4", "#79B8FF"),
    "string": ("#9A3B12", "#E5A66B"),
    "number": ("#1E6B30", "#9CD67E"),
    "mark": ("#FFE27A", "#6E5B10"),       # the followed variable
    "into": ("#BFDDF7", "#1E4A6E"),       # computed from it
    "from": ("#E3D2F7", "#4F3A70"),       # what it is computed from
    "label": ("#59606C", "#9AA1AB"),
    "message": ("#7D8693", "#8E949C"),
}


def _theme():
    k = 1 if dark_theme() else 0
    return {name: QtGui.QColor(v[k]) for name, v in _COLORS.items()}


def _font():
    """The font IDA's pseudocode uses, when it can be found; a fixed-width one otherwise."""
    f = None
    try:
        from .qt import qwidget
        w = qwidget(ida_kernwin.find_widget("Pseudocode-A"))
        if w is not None:
            for c in [w] + w.findChildren(QtWidgets.QWidget):
                cf = c.font()
                if QtGui.QFontInfo(cf).fixedPitch():
                    f = QtGui.QFont(cf)
                    break
    except Exception:
        f = None
    if f is None:
        f = QtGui.QFontDatabase.systemFont(QtGui.QFontDatabase.FixedFont)
        f.setPointSize(max(f.pointSize(), 10))
    return f


def _spans(text, th):
    """C text in coloured pieces: (text, colour, bold)."""
    out = []
    for m in _TOKEN.finditer(text):
        t = m.group(0)
        if t.startswith('"'):
            out.append((t, th["string"], False))
        elif t[0].isdigit():
            out.append((t, th["number"], False))
        elif t in _KEYWORDS:
            out.append((t, th["keyword"], True))
        else:
            out.append((t, th["text"], False))
    return out


def _rx(names):
    names = sorted((n for n in names if n), key=len, reverse=True)
    if not names:
        return None
    return re.compile(r"(?<![\w])(?:%s)(?![\w])" % "|".join(map(re.escape, names)))


def _outside_strings(rx, text):
    if rx is None:
        return
    strings = [m.span() for m in _STRING.finditer(text)]
    for m in rx.finditer(text):
        if not any(a <= m.start() < b for a, b in strings):
            yield m


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- items
class _Holder(QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem):
    """Holds one chart's shapes and lines; a called chart's is scaled into its panel."""

    def __init__(self, parent=None):
        QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem.__init__(self, parent)
        self.setFlag(QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem.ItemHasNoContents, True)

    def boundingRect(self):
        return QtCore.QRectF()

    def paint(self, p, opt, widget=None):
        pass


class _Shape(QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem):
    """One box of the chart: its outline by kind, its lines of text. A call of one of the
    program's own functions can open that function's chart (ea; ⊕ beside it)."""

    def __init__(self, frame, box, w, h):
        QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem.__init__(self, frame.holder)
        self.frame, self.view, self.box, self.w, self.h = frame, frame.view, box, w, h
        self.lit = set()                  # its lines that are lit
        self.rows = self.view.rows(box, w, h)  # [(line id, text, x, baseline y)]
        self.line = box.callee()
        self.call = self.line.call if self.line is not None else None
        self.ea = None
        if (self.call is not None and frame.depth < nest.MAX_DEPTH and self.call[0] not in frame.eas
                and frame.view.provider is not None):
            self.ea = self.call[0]
        self.panel, self.state = None, None      # its open chart; "queued" / "failed"
        if self.ea is None:                       # a call that opens shows a preview instead
            self.setToolTip(T("Click: the way to this step. Double-click a variable to follow it, "
                              "a function to go to it; Enter jumps to the line.",
                              "单击：走到这一步的路。双击变量跟踪它，双击函数名跳过去；回车跳到这一行。"))

    @property
    def on(self):
        return bool(self.lit) or self.box.kind in ("start", "end")

    def boundingRect(self):
        return QtCore.QRectF(-2, -12, self.w + 20, self.h + 14)

    def outline(self):
        w, h, k = self.w, self.h, self.box.kind
        path = QtGui.QPainterPath()
        if k in ("decision", "switch"):
            path.addPolygon(QtGui.QPolygonF([QtCore.QPointF(w / 2, 0), QtCore.QPointF(w, h / 2),
                                             QtCore.QPointF(w / 2, h), QtCore.QPointF(0, h / 2),
                                             QtCore.QPointF(w / 2, 0)]))
        elif k == "io":
            s = self.view.slant(h)
            path.addPolygon(QtGui.QPolygonF([QtCore.QPointF(s, 0), QtCore.QPointF(w, 0),
                                             QtCore.QPointF(w - s, h), QtCore.QPointF(0, h),
                                             QtCore.QPointF(s, 0)]))
        elif k in ("start", "end", "return"):
            path.addRoundedRect(QtCore.QRectF(0, 0, w, h), h / 2, h / 2)
        elif k == "connector":
            path.addEllipse(QtCore.QRectF(0, 0, w, h))
        else:
            path.addRect(QtCore.QRectF(0, 0, w, h))
        return path

    def paint(self, p, opt, widget=None):
        th, view, frame = self.view.th, self.view, self.frame
        p.setRenderHint(QtGui.QPainter.Antialiasing)
        pen = QtGui.QPen(th["line"] if self.on else th["faded_line"], 1.3)
        p.setPen(pen)
        fill = th["fill"]
        if frame.goal == self.box.id:             # the step whose way is shown
            fill = QtGui.QColor(th["mark"])
            fill.setAlpha(150)
            pen.setWidthF(2.2)
            p.setPen(pen)
        p.setBrush(fill)
        p.drawPath(self.outline())
        if self.box.kind == "call":
            p.drawLine(QtCore.QPointF(BAR, 0), QtCore.QPointF(BAR, self.h))
            p.drawLine(QtCore.QPointF(self.w - BAR, 0), QtCore.QPointF(self.w - BAR, self.h))
        if self.ea is not None:
            self._badge(p, th)
        fm = view.fm
        for lid, text, x, y in self.rows:
            lit = lid in self.lit or self.box.kind in ("start", "end")
            for a, b, colour in frame.marked(lid, text):
                x0 = x + fm.horizontalAdvance(text[:a])
                r = QtCore.QRectF(x0 - 1.5, y - fm.ascent() - 1, fm.horizontalAdvance(text[a:b]) + 3,
                                  fm.height() + 2)
                p.setPen(QtCore.Qt.NoPen)
                halves = ((r, colour),) if colour != "both" else (       # from on the left, into on the right
                    (QtCore.QRectF(r.left(), r.top(), r.width() / 2, r.height()), "from"),
                    (QtCore.QRectF(r.center().x(), r.top(), r.width() / 2, r.height()), "into"))
                for part, col in halves:
                    c = QtGui.QColor(th[col])
                    if not lit:
                        c.setAlpha(90)
                    p.setBrush(c)
                    if len(halves) == 1:
                        p.drawRoundedRect(part, 2.5, 2.5)
                    else:
                        p.drawRect(part)
            cx = x
            for t, col, bold in _spans(text, th):
                p.setFont(view.bold if bold else view.font)
                p.setPen(col if lit else th["faded_text"])
                p.drawText(QtCore.QPointF(cx, y), t)
                cx += (view.fmb if bold else fm).horizontalAdvance(t)

    def _badge(self, p, th):
        """⊕ at the top-right corner: zoom in here to open the function; … while it is being
        made, × when it cannot be decompiled."""
        c = QtCore.QPointF(self.w + 8, -4)
        col = th["label"] if self.on else th["faded_text"]
        if self.state in ("queued", "failed"):
            p.setFont(self.view.label_font)
            p.setPen(col)
            p.drawText(QtCore.QRectF(c.x() - 8, c.y() - 8, 16, 16), QtCore.Qt.AlignCenter,
                       "…" if self.state == "queued" else "×")
            return
        p.setPen(QtGui.QPen(col, 1.1))
        p.setBrush(th["fill"])
        p.drawEllipse(c, 5.0, 5.0)
        p.drawLine(QtCore.QPointF(c.x() - 2.8, c.y()), QtCore.QPointF(c.x() + 2.8, c.y()))
        p.drawLine(QtCore.QPointF(c.x(), c.y() - 2.8), QtCore.QPointF(c.x(), c.y() + 2.8))

    def row_at(self, pos):
        """(line id, text, x of the text, baseline) of the text row at item position pos."""
        fm = self.view.fm
        for row in self.rows:
            lid, text, x, y = row
            if y - fm.ascent() - 2 <= pos.y() <= y + fm.descent() + 2:
                return row
        return None

    def token_at(self, pos):
        row = self.row_at(pos)
        if row is None:
            return None, None
        lid, text, x, y = row
        fm = self.view.fm
        # the chart's own names first: Hex-Rays plugins name variables Key_saved_to:_%s_n
        for m in list(self.frame.names_in(text)) + list(_IDENT.finditer(text)):
            a = x + fm.horizontalAdvance(text[:m.start()])
            b = x + fm.horizontalAdvance(text[:m.end()])
            if a - 2 <= pos.x() <= b + 2:
                return lid, m.group(0)
        return lid, None


class _Flow(QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem):
    """One line of the chart: corners, an arrow at its end, yes / no / a case at its start."""

    def __init__(self, frame, path):
        QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem.__init__(self, frame.holder)
        self.view, self.p = frame.view, path
        self.on = True
        self.qpath = QtGui.QPainterPath(QtCore.QPointF(*path.pts[0]))
        for pt in path.pts[1:]:
            self.qpath.lineTo(QtCore.QPointF(*pt))
        self.text = {"yes": T("yes", "是"), "no": T("no", "否")}.get(path.label, path.label)
        self.setZValue(-1)

    def boundingRect(self):
        r = self.qpath.boundingRect().adjusted(-8, -8, 8, 8)
        if self.text:
            x, y = self.p.pts[0]
            r = r.united(QtCore.QRectF(x - 160, y - 24, 320, 48))
        return r

    def paint(self, p, opt, widget=None):
        th = self.view.th
        col = th["line"] if self.on else th["faded_line"]
        p.setRenderHint(QtGui.QPainter.Antialiasing)
        p.setPen(QtGui.QPen(col, 1.3, QtCore.Qt.SolidLine, QtCore.Qt.FlatCap, QtCore.Qt.MiterJoin))
        p.setBrush(QtCore.Qt.NoBrush)
        p.drawPath(self.qpath)
        pts = self.p.pts
        if self.p.arrow and len(pts) > 1:
            (x1, y1), (x2, y2) = pts[-2], pts[-1]
            d = math.hypot(x2 - x1, y2 - y1) or 1
            ux, uy = (x2 - x1) / d, (y2 - y1) / d
            a, b = 9.0, 4.0
            p.setPen(QtCore.Qt.NoPen)
            p.setBrush(col)
            p.drawPolygon(QtGui.QPolygonF([
                QtCore.QPointF(x2, y2),
                QtCore.QPointF(x2 - ux * a - uy * b, y2 - uy * a + ux * b),
                QtCore.QPointF(x2 - ux * a + uy * b, y2 - uy * a - ux * b)]))
        if self.text and len(pts) > 1:
            f = self.view.label_font
            fm = QtGui.QFontMetricsF(f)
            (x1, y1), (x2, y2) = pts[0], pts[1]
            w = fm.horizontalAdvance(self.text)
            if abs(y1 - y2) < 0.5:                       # leaves sideways: above the line
                x = x1 + 6 if x2 > x1 else x1 - 6 - w
                y = y1 - 5
            else:                     # leaves downwards: on its left (what joins comes from the right)
                x, y = x1 - 6 - w, y1 + fm.ascent() + 2
            p.setFont(f)
            p.setPen(th["label"] if self.on else th["faded_text"])
            p.drawText(QtCore.QPointF(x, y), self.text)


class _Panel(QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem):
    """A called function's chart, opened over its call: a call box (double sides) around it."""

    def __init__(self, view, parent):
        QtWidgets.QGraphicsItem.__init__(self, parent)
        self.view, self.w, self.h = view, 0.0, 0.0
        self.frame = None                          # the chart inside
        self.setZValue(10)                         # over the calling chart's shapes and lines

    def boundingRect(self):
        return QtCore.QRectF(-1, -1, self.w + 2, self.h + 2)

    def paint(self, p, opt, widget=None):
        th = self.view.th
        p.setRenderHint(QtGui.QPainter.Antialiasing)
        p.setPen(QtGui.QPen(th["line"], 1.3))
        p.setBrush(th["fill"])
        p.drawRect(QtCore.QRectF(0, 0, self.w, self.h))
        p.drawLine(QtCore.QPointF(BAR, 0), QtCore.QPointF(BAR, self.h))
        p.drawLine(QtCore.QPointF(self.w - BAR, 0), QtCore.QPointF(self.w - BAR, self.h))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- one chart
class _Frame:
    """One function's chart in the drawing: the main one at the top, a called one in the
    panel over its call (shape). scale: drawing units per chart unit; eas: the functions open
    from the top down to this one (a call of one of them does not open again)."""

    def __init__(self, view, chart, holder, depth=0, scale=1.0, eas=(), entry=None, shape=None):
        self.view, self.chart, self.holder = view, chart, holder
        self.depth, self.scale, self.eas = depth, scale, tuple(eas)
        self.entry, self.shape, self.panel = entry, shape, None
        self.goal = None                          # the shape whose way is shown, if any
        self.chosen, self._mark_rx = (), None
        self.marks = {}                           # {line id: {name: "from" | "into" | "both"}}
        self.args = {}                            # {call line id: arguments the light goes into}
        self._var = (set(), (), "", {})           # what the variable lights: back to it after a way
        self._ref_rx = _rx(chart.refs)
        self._names = {v: k for k, v in chart.refs.items() if v[0] == "var"}
        sizes = {b.id: view.measure(b) for b in chart.boxes}
        pos, paths, (W, H) = chart.layout(sizes)
        self.shapes = {}
        for b in chart.boxes:
            if b.id in pos:
                it = _Shape(self, b, *sizes[b.id])
                it.setPos(*pos[b.id])
                self.shapes[b.id] = it
        self.flows = [_Flow(self, p) for p in paths]
        self.paths, self.size = paths, (W, H)
        self.calls = [s for s in self.shapes.values() if s.ea is not None]

    @property
    def name(self):
        title = self.chart.boxes[0].lines[0].text if self.chart.boxes else "?"
        return title.split("(")[0]

    def chain(self):
        out, f = [], self
        while f is not None:
            out.append(f)
            f = f.shape.frame if f.shape is not None else None
        return out[::-1]

    def var_name(self, n):
        return self._names.get(("var", n))

    # ---- names in the text ----
    def names_in(self, text):
        """The chart's variables, globals and functions named in a text row (outside strings)."""
        return _outside_strings(self._ref_rx, text)

    def marked(self, lid, text):
        """[(start, end, colour)] of the marked names in a text row of line lid: the chosen
        names wherever they are, the others on the lines where they take part."""
        here = self.marks.get(lid, {})
        out = []
        for m in _outside_strings(self._mark_rx, text):
            k = "mark" if m.group(0) in self.chosen else here.get(m.group(0))
            if k:
                out.append((m.start(), m.end(), k))
        return out

    # ---- light ----
    def light(self, lit, chosen=(), lines=None, around=None):
        """lit: chart line ids drawn in full; lines: the indexes of the lines between shapes
        drawn in full (None: those that touch a lit shape); around: {line id: {name: "from" |
        "into" | "both"}} marked next to the chosen names."""
        self.chosen = tuple(chosen)
        self.marks = {lid: {n: k for n, k in m.items() if n not in self.chosen}
                      for lid, m in (around or {}).items()}
        self._mark_rx = _rx(set(self.chosen).union(*(m.keys() for m in self.marks.values())))
        for it in self.shapes.values():
            it.lit = {line.id for line in it.box.lines if line.id in lit}
            it.update()
        for i, f in enumerate(self.flows):
            if lines is not None:
                f.on = i in lines
            else:
                ends = [self.shapes.get(b) for b in (f.p.src, f.p.dst) if b is not None]
                f.on = any(s is not None and s.lit for s in ends)
            f.setZValue(-1 if f.on else -2)
            f.update()

    def set_var(self, lit, chosen=(), message="", around=None, args=None):
        self.args = dict(args or {})
        self._var = (set(lit), tuple(chosen), message, dict(around or {}))
        self.goal = None
        self.light(lit, chosen, around=around)

    def all_lit(self):
        self.set_var({line.id for line in self.chart.lines})

    def show_way(self, box):
        """The way from the start to shape `box`: every shape the flow can pass on its way
        there and the exits it takes; the rest faded. None: back to the variable. Returns the
        message to show."""
        if box is None:
            if self.goal is None:
                return None
            self.goal = None
            lit, chosen, message, around = self._var
            self.light(lit, chosen, around=around)
            return message
        on, lines = route(self.paths, self.chart.boxes[0].id, box, self.chart.jumps)
        self.goal = box
        self.light({line.id for b in on for line in self.chart.boxes[b].lines}, (), lines)
        back = ", ".join(self._var[1])
        return T("Esc or click empty space: back to %s" % (back or "the variable"),
                 "Esc 或单击空白处：返回 %s" % (back or "变量高亮"))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the view
class GraphView(QtWidgets.QGraphicsView):
    """A function's flowchart, with the charts of the calls zoomed into; focus() lights the
    lines of the followed variable. provider makes the called charts (see flowview._Callees)."""

    def __init__(self, parent, on_key, on_open, provider=None):
        QtWidgets.QGraphicsView.__init__(self, parent)
        self.on_key, self.on_open, self.provider = on_key, on_open, provider
        self.setScene(QtWidgets.QGraphicsScene(self))
        self.setRenderHints(QtGui.QPainter.Antialiasing | QtGui.QPainter.TextAntialiasing)
        self.setDragMode(QtWidgets.QGraphicsView.ScrollHandDrag)
        self.setTransformationAnchor(QtWidgets.QGraphicsView.NoAnchor)   # _zoom keeps the point
        self.setFrameShape(QtWidgets.QFrame.NoFrame)
        self.setFocusPolicy(QtCore.Qt.StrongFocus)
        self.setHorizontalScrollBarPolicy(QtCore.Qt.ScrollBarAlwaysOff)
        self.setVerticalScrollBarPolicy(QtCore.Qt.ScrollBarAlwaysOff)
        self.root, self.frames, self.active = None, [], None
        self.target = None
        self.content = QtCore.QRectF()           # where the drawing is, inside the canvas
        self._anim = None
        # messages sit on the view, not on the viewport: what is on the viewport is moved
        # pixel by pixel when the drawing is dragged
        self.note = QtWidgets.QLabel(self)
        self.legend = QtWidgets.QLabel(self)      # what the colours mean, top left
        for lab in (self.note, self.legend):
            lab.setAttribute(QtCore.Qt.WA_TransparentForMouseEvents)
            lab.move(14, 8)
            lab.hide()
        self.legend.setTextFormat(QtCore.Qt.RichText)
        self._press = self._click_at = None
        self._click = QtCore.QTimer(self)         # a click waits to see it is not a double-click
        self._click.setSingleShot(True)
        self._click.timeout.connect(self._clicked)
        self._queue = []                          # calls whose charts are to be made, one a turn
        self._loader = QtCore.QTimer(self)
        self._loader.setSingleShot(True)
        self._loader.timeout.connect(self._load_next)
        self._nest_later = QtCore.QTimer(self)    # after a drag: which calls are open
        self._nest_later.setSingleShot(True)
        self._nest_later.timeout.connect(self._update_nest)
        # a picture of the called chart while the mouse rests on its call
        self.peek = QtWidgets.QFrame(self)
        self.peek.setAttribute(QtCore.Qt.WA_TransparentForMouseEvents)
        lay = QtWidgets.QVBoxLayout(self.peek)
        lay.setContentsMargins(8, 6, 8, 8)
        lay.setSpacing(4)
        self.peek_title, self.peek_img = QtWidgets.QLabel(), QtWidgets.QLabel()
        lay.addWidget(self.peek_title)
        lay.addWidget(self.peek_img)
        self.peek.hide()
        self._peek_on, self._peek_cache, self._peek_pos = None, {}, QtCore.QPoint()
        self._peek_timer = QtCore.QTimer(self)
        self._peek_timer.setSingleShot(True)
        self._peek_timer.timeout.connect(self._show_peek)
        self.viewport().setMouseTracking(True)
        self.restyle()

    # the main chart's state, as before charts could open inside calls
    chart = property(lambda self: self.root.chart if self.root else None)
    shapes = property(lambda self: self.root.shapes if self.root else {})
    flows = property(lambda self: self.root.flows if self.root else [])
    paths = property(lambda self: self.root.paths if self.root else [])
    goal = property(lambda self: self.root.goal if self.root else None)
    marks = property(lambda self: self.root.marks if self.root else {})
    chosen = property(lambda self: ", ".join(self.root.chosen) if self.root else "")

    def restyle(self):
        self.th = _theme()
        self.font = _font()
        self.bold = QtGui.QFont(self.font)
        self.bold.setBold(True)
        self.label_font = QtGui.QFont(self.font)
        self.label_font.setPointSizeF(max(self.font.pointSizeF() - 1, 8))
        self.fm, self.fmb = QtGui.QFontMetricsF(self.font), QtGui.QFontMetricsF(self.bold)
        self.setBackgroundBrush(self.th["bg"])
        self.note.setFont(self.font)
        self.legend.setFont(self.label_font)
        self.peek_title.setFont(self.label_font)
        self.peek.setStyleSheet("QFrame { background: %s; border: 1px solid %s; border-radius: 5px; } "
                                "QLabel { border: none; color: %s; }"
                                % (self.th["fill"].name(), self.th["line"].name(), self.th["label"].name()))
        self._peek_cache = {}
        bg = self.th["bg"]
        for lab, col in ((self.note, self.th["message"]), (self.legend, self.th["text"])):
            lab.setStyleSheet("color: %s; background: rgba(%d, %d, %d, 235); padding: 4px 8px; "
                              "border: 1px solid %s; border-radius: 4px;"
                              % (col.name(), bg.red(), bg.green(), bg.blue(),
                                 self.th["faded_line"].name()))

    def say(self, text):
        self.note.setText(text or "")
        self.note.adjustSize()
        self.note.setVisible(bool(text))
        self._place()

    def _place(self):
        """The legend in the top-left corner, a message under it."""
        y = 8
        if self.legend.isVisible():
            self.legend.move(14, y)
            self.legend.raise_()
            y += self.legend.height() + 6
        self.note.move(14, y)
        self.note.raise_()

    def _legend(self):
        """Say what the colours mean in the chart being looked at: its variable's, or a
        clicked step's; for a called chart, which calls lead there."""
        th, rows = self.th, []
        fr = self.active or self.root
        if fr is None:
            return

        def chip(colour, text):
            return ('<span style="background-color:%s; color:%s">&nbsp;%s&nbsp;</span>'
                    % (th[colour].name(), th["text"].name(), html.escape(text)))

        def square(colour):
            return '<span style="color:%s">&#9632;</span>' % th[colour].name()
        if fr is not self.root:
            rows.append(" › ".join(html.escape(f.name) for f in fr.chain()))
        if fr.goal is not None:
            rows.append(square("mark") + " " + T("selected step", "选中步骤"))
            rows.append(square("line") + " " + T("path", "经过路径") + "&nbsp;&nbsp;" +
                        square("faded_line") + " " + T("not on the path", "未经过"))
        elif fr.chosen:
            kinds = {k for m in fr.marks.values() for k in m.values()}
            rows.append(chip("mark", ", ".join(fr.chosen)) + " " + T("current variable", "当前变量"))
            if "from" in kinds:
                rows.append(chip("from", "▲") + " " + T("source", "来源"))
            if "into" in kinds:
                rows.append(chip("into", "▼") + " " + T("destination", "去向"))
            if "both" in kinds:
                rows.append(chip("from", "▲") + chip("into", "▼") + " " +
                            T("source and destination", "来源兼去向"))
            rows.append(square("line") + " " + T("related steps", "相关步骤") + "&nbsp;&nbsp;" +
                        square("faded_line") + " " + T("other steps", "其余步骤"))
        self.legend.setText("<br>".join(rows))
        self.legend.adjustSize()
        self.legend.setVisible(bool(rows))
        self._place()

    # ---- sizes ----
    def slant(self, h):
        return min(16.0, h * 0.35)

    def _text_size(self, box):
        rows = [t for line in box.lines for t in line.text.split("\n")]
        w = max((self.fm.horizontalAdvance(t) for t in rows), default=10)
        h = len(rows) * self.fm.lineSpacing() - self.fm.leading()
        return w, h

    def measure(self, box):
        tw, th = self._text_size(box)
        k = box.kind
        if k in ("decision", "switch"):
            p, q = tw / 2 + 6, th / 2 + 3
            b = max(p / RATIO + q, 22.0)
            return 2 * RATIO * b, 2 * b
        h = th + 2 * PAD_Y
        if k == "io":
            return tw + 2 * PAD_X + 2 * self.slant(h), h
        if k == "call":
            return tw + 2 * PAD_X + 2 * BAR, h
        if k in ("start", "end", "return"):
            return max(tw + 2 * PAD_X + h * 0.6, 72), h
        if k == "connector":
            d = max(tw + 12, th + 12, 28)
            return d, d
        return tw + 2 * PAD_X, h

    def rows(self, box, w, h):
        """Where each row of a box's text goes: [(line id, text, x, baseline)]."""
        fm = self.fm
        rows = [(line.id, t) for line in box.lines for t in line.text.split("\n")]
        tw, th = self._text_size(box)
        y = (h - th) / 2 + fm.ascent()
        out = []
        centred = box.kind in ("decision", "switch", "start", "end", "return", "connector")
        left = PAD_X + (self.slant(h) if box.kind == "io" else BAR if box.kind == "call" else 0)
        for lid, t in rows:
            x = (w - fm.horizontalAdvance(t)) / 2 if centred else left
            out.append((lid, t, x, y))
            y += fm.lineSpacing()
        return out

    # ---- building ----
    def build(self, chart):
        """Lay out and draw a chart; the drawing stays until build() is called again."""
        self._hide_peek()
        self._peek_cache = {}
        self._queue = []
        self._loader.stop()
        self.scene().clear()
        if self.provider is not None:
            self.provider.clear()
        holder = _Holder()
        self.scene().addItem(holder)
        self.root = _Frame(self, chart, holder, eas=(getattr(chart, "ea", None),))
        self.frames, self.active = [self.root], None
        W, H = self.root.size
        self.content = QtCore.QRectF(0, 0, W, H)
        self.resetTransform()
        self._canvas()

    def _extent(self):
        r = QtCore.QRectF(0, 0, *self.root.size) if self.root else QtCore.QRectF()
        for f in self.frames:
            if f.panel is not None and f.panel.isVisible():
                r = r.united(f.panel.sceneBoundingRect())
        return r

    def _canvas(self, z=None):
        """A canvas, not a page: it reaches a window's size past the drawing on every side, so
        any part of the drawing can be dragged to any spot on screen, but not out of sight."""
        if self.content.isEmpty():
            return
        z = z or self.transform().m11()
        vp = self.viewport().size()
        mx, my = max(vp.width() - KEEP, 0) / z, max(vp.height() - KEEP, 0) / z
        c = self.mapToScene(self.viewport().rect().center())
        self.scene().setSceneRect(self.content.adjusted(-mx, -my, mx, my))
        self.centerOn(c)

    def _zmax(self):
        """Further in, the deeper the open charts are drawn."""
        deep = min([f.scale for f in self.frames if f.panel is None or f.panel.isVisible()] or [1.0])
        return ZOOM[1] / deep

    def _zoom(self, f, at=None):
        """Scale by f within ZOOM, keeping the drawing's point at viewport position `at` (the
        mouse; the middle when it is outside) where it is."""
        z = self.transform().m11()
        f = max(ZOOM[0] / z, min(self._zmax() / z, f))
        if abs(f - 1) < 1e-4:
            return
        if at is None:
            at = self.viewport().mapFromGlobal(QtGui.QCursor.pos())
            if not self.viewport().rect().contains(at):
                at = self.viewport().rect().center()
        at = QtCore.QPoint(int(round(at.x())), int(round(at.y())))
        keep = self.mapToScene(at)
        self._canvas(min(z, z * f))             # room for that point to stay put
        self.scale(f, f)
        self._canvas()
        mid = self.mapToScene(self.viewport().rect().center())
        self.centerOn(mid + (keep - self.mapToScene(at)))
        self._update_nest()

    # ---- calls opened inside calls ----
    def _reach(self, sh):
