`tests/test_simd.py`:

```py
"""Intrinsic folding: nested MMX/SSE calls read as one plain expression."""
from fakes import FakeProgram, hcall, num, other, preorder, var

from ibr.core.notes import collect_notes
from ibr.core.simd import _clip, _operand


def notes(e):
    by_ea, by_idx = collect_notes(FakeProgram(), preorder(e))
    return by_idx


def test_rotate_then_bytewise_add():
    # the README example from Butterfly
    e = hcall("_m_paddb", hcall("_m_por", hcall("_m_psllqi", var("v30"), num(1)),
                                hcall("_m_psrlqi", var("v30"), num(0x3F))), other("v42[0]"))
    by_idx = notes(e)
    assert list(by_idx.values()) == ["op: rotl(v30, 1) + v42[0] (+ per byte, no carry between bytes)"]
    assert list(by_idx) == [e.index]            # the folded inner calls get no note of their own


def test_same_operator_chain_is_flat():
    v39, v29 = var("v39"), var("v29")
    e = hcall("_mm_add_epi8", hcall("_mm_add_epi8", hcall("_mm_add_epi8", v39, v39), v39), v29)
    assert list(notes(e).values()) == ["op: v39 + v39 + v39 + v29 (+ per byte, no carry between bytes)"]


def test_xor_with_itself_is_zeroing():
    e = hcall("_mm_xor_si128", var("v1"), var("v1"))
    assert list(notes(e).values()) == ["op: v1 ^ v1 = 0 (zeroing)"]


def test_pack_names_the_target_type():
    e = hcall("_mm_packus_epi16", var("a"), var("b"))
    assert list(notes(e).values()) == ["op: pack_u8(a, b) (each 16-bit lane clamped to 0..255)"]


def test_bitwise_on_whole_register_has_no_lane_rule():
    e = hcall("_mm_or_si128", var("a"), var("b"))
    assert list(notes(e).values()) == ["op: a | b"]


def test_operand_brackets_only_when_needed():
    assert _operand(("a | b", False), "|", True) == "a | b"
    assert _operand(("a & b", False), "|") == "(a & b)"
    assert _operand(("*(__m128i *)((char *)&a17 + v40)", False), "&") == "*(__m128i *)((char *)&a17 + v40)"
    assert _operand(("a - b", False), "-", True) == "a - b"
    assert _operand(("a - b", False), "-") == "(a - b)"


def test_clip_cuts_at_an_operator_and_stays_balanced():
    chain = " | ".join("(*(a3 + %d) & v23[%d])" % (2 * i, 2 * i + 7) for i in range(12))
    out = _clip(chain)
    assert len(out) <= 140 and out.endswith("| ...")
    assert out.count("(") == out.count(")")
    pk = "pack_i8(" + ", ".join("*((char *)this + %d) == -1 ? ~0 : 0" % (16 * i) for i in range(6)) + ")"
    out = _clip(pk)
    assert out.endswith("...)") and out.count("(") == out.count(")")

```

`tests/test_undo.py`:

```py
"""Run then Undo on a fake database: the plugin's changes go, the user's stay."""
from fakes import FakeDecompiler, FakeProgram, FakeSignatures

from ibr.app.annotate import annotate_all, annotate_math
from ibr.app.signatures import apply_signatures
from ibr.app.undo import undo
from ibr.core.journal import Journal

R = {"kind": "static", "ranking": [("2.39", 1.0, 10, 10)], "confidence": "high", "arch": "x86_64",
     "best": "glibc 2.39", "toolchain": None}


def setup():
    prog = FakeProgram(names={0x10: "sub_10", 0x20: "sub_20", 0x30: "sub_30"})
    sigs = FakeSignatures(prog, ["glibc_2.39_amd64.sig"],
                          {"glibc_2.39_amd64": {0x10: "printf", 0x20: "malloc"}},
                          protos={"malloc": "void *(size_t)"})
    return prog, sigs, FakeDecompiler(), Journal()


def run(prog, sigs, journal):
    apply_signatures(prog, sigs, journal, R)
    annotate_all(prog, journal)


def test_run_twice_then_undo_restores_the_original():
    prog, sigs, dec, j = setup()
    prog.fcmts[0x10] = "my note"
    before = (dict(prog.names), dict(prog.fcmts), dict(prog.types), set(prog.lib))
    run(prog, sigs, j)
    assert prog.names[0x10] == "printf"
    assert prog.fcmts[0x10] == "libc: formatted output to stdout\nmy note"
    assert prog.types[0x20] == "void *(size_t)"
    run(prog, sigs, j)
    undo(prog, dec, j)
    assert prog.names == before[0]
    assert {k: v for k, v in prog.fcmts.items() if v} == before[1]   # empty = no comment
    assert prog.types == before[2] and prog.lib == before[3]


def test_user_edits_after_run_survive_undo():
    prog, sigs, dec, j = setup()
    run(prog, sigs, j)
    prog.names[0x20] = "my_alloc"                                   # renamed by the user
    prog.fcmts[0x10] = prog.fcmts[0x10] + "\nmine"                 # comment edited by the user
    prog.types[0x20] = "char *(int)"                                # retyped by the user
    undo(prog, dec, j)
    assert prog.names[0x20] == "my_alloc"
    assert prog.fcmts[0x10] == "mine"
    assert prog.types[0x20] == "char *(int)"
    assert prog.names[0x10] == "sub_10"


def test_guessed_type_comes_back_guessed():
    prog, sigs, dec, j = setup()
    prog.types[0x20] = "__int64 (int)"                              # IDA's guess, not a user type
    run(prog, sigs, j)
    assert 0x20 in prog.user_types
    undo(prog, dec, j)
    assert prog.types[0x20] == "__int64 (int)" and 0x20 not in prog.user_types


def test_instruction_math_keeps_user_text():
    prog, sigs, dec, j = setup()
    prog.items[0x30] = [(0x31, "xor", "eax", "eax", ""), (0x33, "mov", "eax", "1", "")]
    prog.icmts[0x31] = "key loop"
    assert annotate_math(prog, j, 0x30) == 1
    assert annotate_math(prog, j, 0x30) == 0                        # second run: nothing new
    assert prog.icmts[0x31] == "op: eax = 0  | key loop"
    assert annotate_math(prog, j, 0x99) == -1
    undo(prog, dec, j)
    assert prog.icmts[0x31] == "key loop"


def test_pseudocode_comments():
    prog, sigs, dec, j = setup()
    loc = (0x14, 74)
    dec.cmts[(0x10, loc)] = "op: x"
    j.record("pcmts", (0x10,) + loc, "", "op: x")
    dec.cmts[(0x10, (0x18, 74))] = "op: y  | mine"                 # edited by the user since
    j.record("pcmts", (0x10, 0x18, 74), "", "op: y")
    undo(prog, dec, j)
    assert dec.cmts[(0x10, loc)] == "" and dec.cmts[(0x10, (0x18, 74))] == "mine"

```

`tools/build-db.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Build the per-architecture byte-pattern database (FLAIR .pat) for glibc / musl / libgcc.

For every (family, arch, version) it:
  1. picks a package from the Ubuntu pools (archive / ports / old-releases):
       glibc x86_64  libc6-dev_<v>_amd64.deb         -> usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libc.a
       glibc i386    libc6-dev-i386_<v>_amd64.deb    -> usr/lib32/libc.a
       glibc aarch64 libc6-dev_<v>_arm64.deb         -> usr/lib/aarch64-linux-gnu/libc.a
       musl  *       musl-dev_<v>_<arch>.deb         -> usr/lib/musl/lib/libc.a
       libgcc *      libgcc-<N>-dev / lib32gcc-<N>-dev -> libgcc.a, libgcc_eh.a, crtbeginT.o, crtend.o
     (newest Ubuntu revision for the DB; with --corpus the OLDEST one, so the
      regression corpus is linked against a different build than the DB)
     libgcc patterns go to <out>/<arch>/libgcc/<N>.pat: they become FLIRT signatures
     but stay out of the libc fingerprint, which only reads <out>/<arch>/*.pat
  2. extracts libc.a (+ crt objects) with pure Python (ar + tar.{xz,zst,gz})
  3. runs FLAIR `pelf` -> <out>/<arch>/<key>.pat     (key = "2.35" or "musl_1.2.5")

usage:
  python tools/build-db.py --pelf <flair>/pelf.exe [--arch x86_64,i386,aarch64]
                           [--family glibc,musl,libgcc] [--out db] [--cache _cache]
  python tools/build-db.py --corpus --cache _cache      # only fetch + extract (oldest rev)

Then: python tools/build-fingerprint.py _db tools/fingerprint.json
"""
import argparse, io, os, re, subprocess, sys, tarfile, time, urllib.request

GLIBC_VERSIONS = ["2.19", "2.21", "2.23", "2.24", "2.26", "2.27", "2.28", "2.29", "2.30",
                  "2.31", "2.32", "2.33", "2.34", "2.35", "2.36", "2.37", "2.38", "2.39",
                  "2.40", "2.41", "2.42", "2.43", "2.44"]
MUSL_VERSIONS = ["1.1.24", "1.2.2", "1.2.3", "1.2.4", "1.2.5", "1.2.6"]
# GCC majors shipped by Ubuntu 14.04 .. 25.10 (libgcc is linked into every static binary)
GCC_VERSIONS = ["4.8", "5", "7", "9", "10", "11", "12", "13", "14", "15"]
GCC_KEEP = ("libgcc.a", "libgcc_eh.a", "crtbeginT.o", "crtend.o")
GCC_TRIPLE = {"x86_64": "x86_64-linux-gnu", "i386": "x86_64-linux-gnu", "aarch64": "aarch64-linux-gnu"}

ARCHIVE = "http://archive.ubuntu.com/ubuntu/pool"
PORTS = "http://ports.ubuntu.com/ubuntu-ports/pool"
OLD = "http://old-releases.ubuntu.com/ubuntu/pool"

# arch -> (deb arch, glibc package, libc.a dir inside the deb, pools to search)
ARCHES = {
    "x86_64":  ("amd64", "libc6-dev",      "usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu", (ARCHIVE, OLD)),
    "i386":    ("amd64", "libc6-dev-i386", "usr/lib32",                (ARCHIVE, OLD)),
    "aarch64": ("arm64", "libc6-dev",      "usr/lib/aarch64-linux-gnu", (PORTS, OLD)),
}
MUSL_DEB_ARCH = {"x86_64": "amd64", "i386": "i386", "aarch64": "arm64"}
KEEP = ("libc.a", "libc_nonshared.a", "crt1.o", "crti.o", "crtn.o", "rcrt1.o", "Scrt1.o")

# Ubuntu stopped building musl for i386 after 1.1.19 -> fall back to Alpine (x86) there.
ALPINE = "https://dl-cdn.alpinelinux.org/alpine"
ALPINE_REL = {"1.1.24": "v3.12", "1.2.2": "v3.14", "1.2.3": "v3.17", "1.2.4": "v3.19",
              "1.2.5": "v3.21", "1.2.6": "edge"}
ALPINE_ARCH = {"x86_64": "x86_64", "i386": "x86", "aarch64": "aarch64"}


def log(*a):
    print(*a, flush=True)


def fetch(url, dst=None, tries=3):
    last = None
    for i in range(tries):
        try:
            with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=120) as r:
                data = r.read()
            if dst:
                os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst), exist_ok=True)
                with open(dst + ".part", "wb") as f:
                    f.write(data)
                os.replace(dst + ".part", dst)
            return data
        except Exception as e:          # flaky mirrors: retry
            last = e
            time.sleep(2 + 3 * i)
    raise last


_listing_cache = {}

def listing(cache, url):
    """Pool directory listing -> set of .deb names (cached on disk for a day)."""
    if url in _listing_cache:
        return _listing_cache[url]
    fn = os.path.join(cache, "listings", re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9]+", "_", url) + ".html")
    if not (os.path.exists(fn) and time.time() - os.path.getmtime(fn) < 86400):
        fetch(url, fn)
    html = open(fn, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").read()
    names = set(re.findall(r'href="([^"]+\.(?:deb|apk))"', html))
    _listing_cache[url] = names
    return names


def _rev_key(rev):
    """Debian revision -> sortable tuple ('0ubuntu3.15' > '0ubuntu3.2' > '0ubuntu3')."""
    return [int(t) if t.isdigit() else t for t in re.findall(r"\d+|[A-Za-z]+", rev)]


def pick_deb(cache, family, arch, ver, oldest=False):
    """Return (url, debname) of the newest (or oldest) Ubuntu build of <ver>."""
    debarch, pkg, _, pools = ARCHES[arch]
    if family == "libgcc":
        pkg = ("lib32gcc-%s-dev" if arch == "i386" else "libgcc-%s-dev") % ver
        sub = "main/g/gcc-%s" % ver
        rx = re.compile(r"^%s_([^_]+)_%s\.deb$" % (re.escape(pkg), debarch))
        ver = None
    elif family == "musl":
        pkg, debarch, sub = "musl-dev", MUSL_DEB_ARCH[arch], "universe/m/musl"
        pools = (PORTS, OLD) if arch == "aarch64" else (ARCHIVE, OLD)
    else:
        sub = "main/g/glibc"
    if family != "libgcc":
        rx = re.compile(r"^%s_%s(?:-([^_]+))?_%s\.deb$" % (re.escape(pkg), re.escape(ver), debarch))
    cands = []
    for pool in pools:
        base = "%s/%s/" % (pool, sub)
        try:
            names = listing(cache, base)
        except Exception as e:
            log("   listing failed: %s (%s)" % (base, e))
            continue
        for n in names:
            m = rx.match(n)
            if m:
                key = _rev_key(m.group(1) or "")
                if family == "libgcc":
                    # prefer release builds (14.2.0-...) over pre-release snapshots (14-20240412-...)
                    key = [1 if re.match(r"\d+\.\d", m.group(1)) else 0] + key
                cands.append((key, base + n, n))
        if cands and pool != OLD:
            break                       # prefer the live pool; fall back to old-releases
    if not cands and family == "musl" and ver in ALPINE_REL:
        base = "%s/%s/main/%s/" % (ALPINE, ALPINE_REL[ver], ALPINE_ARCH[arch])
        arx = re.compile(r"^musl-dev-%s(?:_git\d+)?-r(\d+)\.apk$" % re.escape(ver))
        try:
            for n in listing(cache, base):
                m = arx.match(n)
                if m:
                    cands.append(([int(m.group(1))], base + n, n))
        except Exception as e:
            log("   listing failed: %s (%s)" % (base, e))
    if not cands:
        return None, None
    cands.sort()
    _, url, name = cands[0] if oldest else cands[-1]
    return url, name


def ar_members(blob):
    if not blob.startswith(b"!<arch>\n"):
        raise ValueError("not an ar archive")
    off = 8
    while off + 60 <= len(blob):
        hdr = blob[off:off + 60]
        name = hdr[:16].decode().strip().rstrip("/")
        size = int(hdr[48:58].decode().strip())
        yield name, blob[off + 60: off + 60 + size]
        off += 60 + size + (size & 1)


def extract(deb_path, libdir, outdir, keep=KEEP):
    """Pull libc.a + crt objects out of a .deb / .apk into outdir. Returns list of files."""
    blob = open(deb_path, "rb").read()
    if deb_path.endswith(".apk"):               # Alpine: concatenated gzip'd tar segments
        import gzip
        data, libdir, mode = gzip.decompress(blob), "usr/lib", "r:"
    else:
        data = next((d for n, d in ar_members(blob) if n.startswith("data.tar")), None)
        mode = "r:*"
    if data is None:
        raise ValueError("no data.tar in " + deb_path)
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    got = []
    with tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(data), mode=mode, ignore_zeros=True) as tf:
        for m in tf:
            p = m.name.lstrip("./")
            if not m.isfile() or os.path.dirname(p) != libdir.strip("/"):
                continue
            if os.path.basename(p) in keep:
                dst = os.path.join(outdir, os.path.basename(p))
                with open(dst, "wb") as f:
                    f.write(tf.extractfile(m).read())
                got.append(dst)
    return got


def libdir_for(family, arch, ver=None):
    if family == "libgcc":
        return "usr/lib/gcc/%s/%s%s" % (GCC_TRIPLE[arch], ver, "/32" if arch == "i386" else "")
    if family == "musl":                        # Debian/Ubuntu musl-dev layout (.apk handled in extract)
        return "usr/lib/%s-linux-musl" % arch
    return ARCHES[arch][2]


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("--arch", default="x86_64,i386,aarch64")
    ap.add_argument("--family", default="glibc,musl,libgcc")
    ap.add_argument("--versions", default="", help="comma list to restrict versions")
    ap.add_argument("--pelf", default=os.environ.get("FLAIR_PELF", ""))
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="_db")
    ap.add_argument("--cache", default="_cache")
    ap.add_argument("--corpus", action="store_true", help="fetch the OLDEST revision, extract only (no pelf)")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    only = set(v for v in args.versions.split(",") if v)
    ok, fail = [], []
    for family in args.family.split(","):
        vers = {"glibc": GLIBC_VERSIONS, "musl": MUSL_VERSIONS, "libgcc": GCC_VERSIONS}[family]
        for arch in args.arch.split(","):
            for ver in vers:
                if only and ver not in only:
                    continue
                key = {"glibc": ver, "musl": "musl_" + ver, "libgcc": "libgcc_" + ver}[family]
                tag = "%s/%s" % (arch, key)
                pat = os.path.join(args.out, arch, *(["libgcc", ver + ".pat"] if family == "libgcc"
                                                     else [key + ".pat"]))
                if not args.corpus and os.path.exists(pat) and os.path.getsize(pat) > 0:
                    log("skip %-18s (exists)" % tag); ok.append(tag); continue
                url, name = pick_deb(args.cache, family, arch, ver, oldest=args.corpus)
                if not url:
                    log("MISS %-18s no package in the Ubuntu pools" % tag); fail.append(tag); continue
                deb = os.path.join(args.cache, "debs", name)
                try:
                    if not os.path.exists(deb):
                        log("get  %-18s %s" % (tag, name))
                        fetch(url, deb)
                    xdir = os.path.join(args.cache, "x", os.path.splitext(name)[0])
                    if family == "libgcc":
                        files = extract(deb, libdir_for(family, arch, ver), xdir, GCC_KEEP)
                        inputs = [f for k in GCC_KEEP for f in files if os.path.basename(f) == k]
                    else:
                        files = extract(deb, libdir_for(family, arch), xdir)
                        inputs = [os.path.join(xdir, "libc.a")]
                    if not inputs or not os.path.exists(inputs[0]):
                        raise RuntimeError("%s not found in package" % ("libgcc.a" if family == "libgcc" else "libc.a"))
                    if args.corpus:
                        log("ok   %-18s %s (%d files)" % (tag, xdir, len(files))); ok.append(tag); continue
                    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(pat), exist_ok=True)
                    if os.path.exists(pat):
                        os.remove(pat)
                    for i, inp in enumerate(inputs):         # -a: append every input to one .pat
                        r = subprocess.run([args.pelf] + (["-a"] if i else []) + [inp, pat],
                                           capture_output=True, text=True)
                    if not os.path.exists(pat) or os.path.getsize(pat) == 0:
                        raise RuntimeError("pelf failed: " + (r.stdout + r.stderr)[-400:])
                    n = sum(1 for _ in open(pat, errors="ignore"))
                    log("ok   %-18s %d patterns  (%s)" % (tag, n, name)); ok.append(tag)
                except Exception as e:
                    log("FAIL %-18s %s" % (tag, e)); fail.append(tag)
    log("done: %d ok, %d failed %s" % (len(ok), len(fail), " ".join(fail)))
    sys.exit(1 if fail and not ok else 0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/build-fingerprint.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""FLAIR patterns (<db>/<arch>/<key>.pat) -> fingerprint database.

An anchor is the longest wildcard-free byte run (10..24 bytes) of a function's leading
32-byte FLAIR pattern. Each libc build is represented by the set of its anchors; at
detection time the build whose anchors appear most completely in the target wins.

Two outputs:
  fingerprint.json  human-readable, {"format": 2, "arches": {arch: {key: [hex, ...]}}}
                    (tools/, not shipped)
  fingerprint.bin   compact form the plugin reads (src/ibr/data/, see _decode_fp in
                    src/ibr/core/fingerprint.py): anchors stored once per arch, versions as
                    varint deltas. Next to out.json when an output path is given.

Bytes the linker fills in or rewrites (TLS IE->LE, relocated AArch64 words; see flair_relax.py) are
wildcarded first, so anchors never span them.

usage: python tools/build-fingerprint.py [db_dir] [out.json] [--no-relax-tls] [--no-relax-got]
"""
import glob, json, os, struct, sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import flair_relax
_LIBC_GLOB = {"x86_64": "libc6-dev_*_amd64", "i386": "libc6-dev-i386_*_amd64", "aarch64": "libc6-dev_*_arm64"}

MIN_RUN = 10    # shortest anchor kept
MAX_ANCH = 24   # anchors are truncated to this many bytes
MAGIC = b"IBIFP2"


def longest_literal_run(tok):
    best, cur = b"", bytearray()
    for i in range(0, len(tok) - 1, 2):
        c = tok[i:i + 2]
        try:
            cur.append(int(c, 16))
        except ValueError:              # ".." wildcard
            if len(cur) > len(best):
                best = bytes(cur)
            cur = bytearray()
    if len(cur) > len(best):
        best = bytes(cur)
    return best[:MAX_ANCH]


def anchors_from_pat(path, tls=None, arch=None, relocs=False):
    out = set()
    with open(path, "r", errors="ignore") as f:
        for line in f:
            line = line.strip()
            if tls:                      # wildcard bytes the linker rewrites (TLS relaxation)
                line = flair_relax.relax_line(line, tls, arch)[0]
            if relocs:                   # ... and GOT-indirect -> direct relaxation
                line = flair_relax.relax_relocs(line, arch)[0]
            if not line or line.startswith("---"):
                continue
            tok = line.split(" ", 1)[0]
            if len(tok) < 2 * MIN_RUN:
                continue
            run = longest_literal_run(tok)
            if len(run) >= MIN_RUN:
                out.add(run.hex())
    return sorted(out)


def _uvarint(n):
    b = bytearray()
    while True:
        x = n & 0x7F
        n >>= 7
        b.append(x | (0x80 if n else 0))
        if not n:
            return bytes(b)


def _str(s):
    s = s.encode()
    return bytes([len(s)]) + s


def _version_key(k):
    fam = 1 if k.startswith("musl") else 0
    nums = [int(x) for x in k.split("_")[-1].split(".") if x.isdigit()]
    return (fam, nums)


def encode_bin(arches):
    out = bytearray(MAGIC)
    out += bytes([len(arches)])
    for arch, vers in sorted(arches.items()):
        table = sorted({a for anchors in vers.values() for a in anchors})
        index = {a: i for i, a in enumerate(table)}
        out += _str(arch) + _uvarint(len(table))
        for a in table:
            raw = bytes.fromhex(a)
            out += bytes([len(raw)]) + raw
        out += _uvarint(len(vers))
        for key in sorted(vers, key=_version_key):
            idx = sorted(index[a] for a in vers[key])
            out += _str(key) + _uvarint(len(idx))
            prev = 0
            for i in idx:
                out += _uvarint(i - prev)
                prev = i
    return bytes(out)


def main():
    here = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("--")]
    db = args[0] if len(args) > 0 else os.path.join(here, "..", "_db")
    out = args[1] if len(args) > 1 else os.path.join(here, "fingerprint.json")
    relax = "--no-relax-tls" not in sys.argv
    relocs = "--no-relax-got" not in sys.argv
    cache = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(db)), "_cache")
    arches = {}
    for arch_dir in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(db, "*"))):
        if not os.path.isdir(arch_dir):
            continue
        arch = os.path.basename(arch_dir)
        tls = None
        if relax and arch in _LIBC_GLOB:
            tls = flair_relax.tls_names(glob.glob(os.path.join(cache, "x", _LIBC_GLOB[arch], "libc.a")))
        for p in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(arch_dir, "*.pat"))):
            key = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(p))[0]
            a = anchors_from_pat(p, tls, arch, relocs)
            if a:
                arches.setdefault(arch, {})[key] = a
        if arch in arches:
            n = len({x for v in arches[arch].values() for x in v})
            print("%-8s %2d builds, %5d distinct anchors" % (arch, len(arches[arch]), n))
    if not arches:
        sys.exit("no <arch>/*.pat under %s - run tools/build-db.py first" % db)
    with open(out, "w") as f:
        json.dump({"format": 2, "arches": arches}, f, separators=(",", ":"))
    blob = encode_bin(arches)
    binpath = (os.path.splitext(out)[0] + ".bin" if len(args) > 1 else
               os.path.join(here, "..", "src", "ibr", "data", "fingerprint.bin"))
    with open(binpath, "wb") as f:
        f.write(blob)
    print("-> %s (%d bytes json), %s (%d bytes)" % (out, os.path.getsize(out), os.path.normpath(binpath), len(blob)))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/flair_relax.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Make FLAIR patterns match code as it looks *after* static linking.

pelf builds patterns from the object files in libc.a and wildcards the bytes a
relocation fills in. Two things make those patterns miss in a linked executable:

1. TLS relaxation (relax_line). libc.a reaches its thread-local variables (errno,
   h_errno, tcache, the locale pointers, ...) through the initial-exec model, e.g.
   on x86-64

       mov rcx, [rip + __libc_errno@gottpoff]      48 8B 0D <rel32>

   and the static linker rewrites that into the local-exec form

       mov rcx, -0x40                              48 C7 C1 <imm32>

   pelf wildcards only the 4 relocated bytes, so the rewritten opcode bytes stay
   literal. Most syscall wrappers (read, mmap, ptrace, alarm...) are affected.

2. Incomplete immediates on AArch64 (relax_relocs). pelf wildcards only part of a
   relocated instruction: ADRP keeps its top byte (which holds immlo, bits 29-30)
   and LDR/STR/ADD keep the byte with the high bits of imm12, although the linker
   fills those bits in too:

       pattern  ......90   binary  600400F0   adrp x0, #0x48f000
       pattern  ....40F9   binary  002446F9   ldr  x0, [x0, #0xc48]

   Such a pattern only matches when those bits happen to be 0. Every instruction
   word pelf left partly wildcarded is therefore wildcarded completely; this also
   covers the TLS (adrp/ldr -> movz/movk) and GOT -> direct rewrites.

On stripped static glibc 2.39 test binaries (scored against the unstripped twin)
this raises correctly named functions from 804 to 858 on x86-64 and from 293 to
495 on AArch64; i386 patterns are unaffected.
"""
import io, re, struct

STT_TLS = 6

# bytes around a relocation offset R that relaxation may rewrite, per arch
#   x86-64: REX + opcode + ModRM before the rel32   -> [R-3, R)
#   i386  : opcode + ModRM before the disp32        -> [R-2, R)
#   AArch64: the whole instruction holding the reloc -> [R, R+4)
_SPAN = {"x86_64": (-3, 0), "i386": (-2, 0), "aarch64": (0, 4)}


def _ar_members(blob):
    if not blob.startswith(b"!<arch>\n"):
        return
    off = 8
    while off + 60 <= len(blob):
        size = int(blob[off + 48:off + 58].decode().strip() or 0)
        yield blob[off + 60:off + 60 + size]
        off += 60 + size + (size & 1)


def _elf_tls_names(obj, out):
    if obj[:4] != b"\x7fELF":
        return
    is64, en = obj[4] == 2, "<" if obj[5] == 1 else ">"
    try:
        if is64:
            shoff = struct.unpack_from(en + "Q", obj, 0x28)[0]
            shentsize, shnum = struct.unpack_from(en + "HH", obj, 0x3A)
        else:
            shoff = struct.unpack_from(en + "I", obj, 0x20)[0]
            shentsize, shnum = struct.unpack_from(en + "HH", obj, 0x2E)
        secs = []
        for i in range(shnum):
            o = shoff + i * shentsize
            if is64:
                _, typ, _, _, off, size, link, _, _, entsz = struct.unpack_from(en + "IIQQQQIIQQ", obj, o)
            else:
                _, typ, _, _, off, size, link, _, _, entsz = struct.unpack_from(en + "IIIIIIIIII", obj, o)
            secs.append((typ, off, size, link, entsz))
        for typ, off, size, link, entsz in secs:
            if typ != 2 or not entsz:                     # SHT_SYMTAB
                continue
            stoff = secs[link][1]
            for j in range(off, off + size, entsz):
                if is64:
                    name, info = struct.unpack_from(en + "IB", obj, j)
                else:
                    name = struct.unpack_from(en + "I", obj, j)[0]
                    info = obj[j + 12]
                if info & 0xF == STT_TLS and name:
                    end = obj.index(b"\x00", stoff + name)
                    out.add(obj[stoff + name:end].decode("latin1"))
    except Exception:
        pass


def tls_names(libc_a_paths):
    """Union of STT_TLS symbol names (defined or referenced) in the given archives."""
    out = set()
    for p in libc_a_paths:
        try:
            blob = open(p, "rb").read()
        except OSError:
            continue
        for obj in _ar_members(blob):
            _elf_tls_names(obj, out)
    return out


_REF = re.compile(r"\^([0-9A-F]{4,8}) (\S+)")


def _parse(line):
    """-> (fields, head bytes, crclen, tail field index or None, tail bytes or None)"""
    f = line.split(" ")
    head = [f[0][i:i + 2] for i in range(0, len(f[0]), 2)]
    crclen, total = int(f[1], 16), int(f[3], 16)
    tail_i = len(f) - 1 if re.fullmatch(r"[0-9A-F.]+", f[-1]) and not f[-1].startswith((":", "^")) \
        and len(f[-1]) == 2 * max(0, total - 32 - crclen) and len(f) > 5 else None
    tail = [f[tail_i][i:i + 2] for i in range(0, len(f[tail_i]), 2)] if tail_i else None
    return f, head, crclen, tail_i, tail


def _wildcard(line, positions, crc_hit="drop"):
    """Wildcard the given module offsets. A position inside the CRC-covered range either
    drops the CRC (crc_hit="drop": its bytes are not in the .pat, so it cannot be
    recomputed) or is skipped (crc_hit="skip")."""
    f, head, crclen, tail_i, tail = _parse(line)
    changed = drop_crc = False
    for p in positions:
        if 0 <= p < len(head):
            changed |= head[p] != ".."
            head[p] = ".."
        elif 32 <= p < 32 + crclen:
            drop_crc |= crc_hit == "drop"
        elif tail is not None and 0 <= p - 32 - crclen < len(tail):
            changed |= tail[p - 32 - crclen] != ".."
            tail[p - 32 - crclen] = ".."
    if not (changed or drop_crc):
        return line, False
    f[0] = "".join(head)
    if drop_crc:
        f[1], f[2] = "00", "0000"
        if tail_i:
            f.pop(tail_i)          # the tail was positioned after the old CRC range
    elif tail_i:
        f[tail_i] = "".join(tail)
    return " ".join(f), True


def _is_pattern(line):
    return bool(line) and not line.startswith("---") and len(line.split(" ")) >= 5


def relax_line(line, tls, arch):
    """TLS IE->LE relaxation. Return (new_line, changed) for one .pat line."""
    if not _is_pattern(line):
        return line, False
    span = _SPAN.get(arch)
    hits = [int(m.group(1), 16) for m in _REF.finditer(line) if m.group(2) in tls]
    if not span or not hits:
        return line, False
    return _wildcard(line, [p for r in hits for p in range(r + span[0], r + span[1])])


# x86-64 instructions whose RIP-relative GOT operand the linker may relax (R_X86_64_
# [REX_]GOTPCRELX): mov -> lea / mov $imm, call|jmp * -> addr32 call|jmp+nop, and
# test/ALU reg,[GOT] -> op $imm,reg. Keyed by opcode; ModRM must be RIP-relative.
_X64_GOTX = {0x8B, 0xFF, 0x85, 0x03, 0x0B, 0x13, 0x1B, 0x23, 0x2B, 0x33, 0x3B}


def relax_relocs(line, arch):
    """Relocated instructions whose encoding pelf only partly wildcards.
      AArch64: any instruction word pelf left partly wildcarded carries a relocation
               (see the module docstring), so the whole 32-bit word is wildcarded.
      x86-64 : a 4-byte wildcard after a RIP-relative ModRM with a GOTPCRELX-
               relaxable opcode gets its opcode (+ REX) bytes wildcarded too
               (mov -> lea / mov $imm, call|jmp * -> direct). No glibc 2.39 test
               function depended on it; it guards other archives."""
    if not _is_pattern(line) or arch not in ("aarch64", "x86_64", "i386"):
        return line, False
    f, head, crclen, tail_i, tail = _parse(line)
    segs = [(0, head)] + ([(32 + crclen, tail)] if tail else [])
    pos = set()
    if arch == "aarch64":
        for base, seq in segs:
            for i, b in enumerate(seq):
                if b == "..":
                    w = (base + i) & ~3
                    pos.update(range(w, w + 4))
        return _wildcard(line, pos, crc_hit="skip")
    for base, seq in segs:
        for i in range(2, len(seq) - 3):
            if seq[i:i + 4] != [".."] * 4 or ".." in (seq[i - 2], seq[i - 1]):
                continue
            op, modrm = int(seq[i - 2], 16), int(seq[i - 1], 16)
            if arch == "i386":
                # R_386_GOT32X: `mov reg, [ebx + sym@GOT]` 8B 83 <disp32> becomes
                # `mov reg, imm32` C7 C0 <imm32> (call/jmp/test/ALU forms likewise);
                # the base+disp32 ModRM (mod=10, no SIB) is what pelf left literal.
                if op in _X64_GOTX and modrm & 0xC0 == 0x80 and modrm & 7 != 4:
                    pos.update((base + i - 2, base + i - 1))
                continue
            if op in _X64_GOTX and modrm & 0xC7 == 0x05:
                pos.update((base + i - 2, base + i - 1))
                if i >= 3 and seq[i - 3] != ".." and 0x40 <= int(seq[i - 3], 16) <= 0x4F:
                    pos.add(base + i - 3)
    return _wildcard(line, pos, crc_hit="skip")


def relax_pat(src, dst, tls, arch):
    n = changed = 0
    with open(src, "r", errors="ignore") as fi, open(dst, "w", newline="\n") as fo:
        for line in fi:
            new, c = relax_line(line.rstrip("\n"), tls, arch)
            fo.write(new + "\n")
            n += 1
            changed += c
    return n, changed

```

`tools/gen-syscalls.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Generate src/ibr/data/syscalls.json (num -> name per arch) from Linux kernel headers.

Run on a Linux box (or WSL) that has libc headers installed:
    sudo apt install libc6-dev gcc-aarch64-linux-gnu
    python3 tools/gen-syscalls.py > src/ibr/data/syscalls.json

x86_64 / i386 come straight from <asm/unistd_{64,32}.h>. aarch64 uses the generic
syscall table (asm-generic/unistd.h) expanded with the __ARCH_WANT_* macros arm64 sets.
"""
import json, re, subprocess, sys

X64 = "/usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu/asm/unistd_64.h"
X86 = "/usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu/asm/unistd_32.h"
A64_WANTS = ["__ARCH_WANT_RENAMEAT", "__ARCH_WANT_NEW_STAT", "__ARCH_WANT_SET_GET_RLIMIT",
             "__ARCH_WANT_TIME32_SYSCALLS", "__ARCH_WANT_SYS_CLONE3", "__ARCH_WANT_MEMFD_SECRET"]


def from_header(path):
    d = {}
    for m in re.finditer(r"#define __NR_(\w+)\s+(\d+)", open(path).read()):
        d[int(m.group(2))] = m.group(1)
    return d


def from_generic():
    cmd = ["gcc", "-E", "-dM"] + ["-D" + w for w in A64_WANTS] + \
          ["-include", "asm-generic/unistd.h", "-"]
    dump = subprocess.run(cmd, input="", capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    defs = dict(re.findall(r"#define (\w+) (.+)", dump))

    def ev(x, depth=0):
        x = x.strip().strip("()")
        if x.isdigit():
            return int(x)
        if x in defs and depth < 12:
            return ev(defs[x], depth + 1)
        return None
    d = {}
    for k, v in defs.items():
        if k.startswith("__NR_") and not k.startswith("__NR3264") and k != "__NR_syscalls":
            val = ev(v)
            if val is not None and val < 2000:
                d[val] = k[5:]
    return d


def main():
    out = {}
    for arch, d in (("x86_64", from_header(X64)), ("i386", from_header(X86)),
                    ("aarch64", from_generic())):
        out[arch] = " ".join("%d:%s" % (n, d[n]) for n in sorted(d))
        print("%-8s %d syscalls" % (arch, len(d)), file=sys.stderr)
    json.dump(out, sys.stdout, separators=(",", ":"))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/make-sigs.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Compile the byte-pattern DB (db/<arch>/*.pat) into FLIRT signatures (.sig).

Before compiling, patterns are rewritten to match code as it looks after static
linking (see flair_relax.py): bytes the linker rewrites when it relaxes initial-exec
TLS accesses (errno, h_errno, locale pointers...) are wildcarded, and on AArch64 so
is every relocated instruction word that pelf only partly wildcarded. Against the
unstripped twin of stripped static glibc 2.39 binaries this takes correctly named
functions from 804 to 855 (x86-64) and from 294 to 495 (AArch64).

Patterns with fewer than --min-literal fixed bytes (leading bytes + CRC-covered bytes
+ tail) are dropped: pelf emits a few that are little more than a function prologue
(AArch64 `_dl_start` is `stp x29,x30,[sp,#-16]!; mov x29,sp` followed by wildcards),
and FLIRT would stamp that name on every function it fails to match otherwise.

Collisions (.exc) are resolved by excluding the conflicting modules; in libc they
are almost all same-byte aliases (htons/ntohs, atoi/atol).

Output names:  glibc_<ver>_<arch>.sig   musl_<ver>_<arch>.sig   libgcc_<gcc major>_<arch>.sig
(libgcc.a + libgcc_eh.a + crtbeginT.o + crtend.o: the GCC runtime every static binary carries)
FLIRT keys signatures by directory, not by the .sig processor byte (IDA's own
android_arm.sig also reports processor 0), so x86 sigs install to sig/pc and
AArch64 sigs to sig/arm.

usage:
  python tools/make-sigs.py --sigmake <flair>/sigmake.exe [--db _db]
         [--arch x86_64,i386,aarch64] [--family glibc,musl] [--out _sigs] [--min-literal N]
         [--install <IDA dir>]          # copy into <IDA>/sig/{pc,arm}
"""
import argparse, glob, os, re, shutil, subprocess, sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import flair_relax

# where build-db.py extracted each arch's libc.a (used to read the real TLS symbol names)
_LIBC_GLOB = {"x86_64": "libc6-dev_*_amd64", "i386": "libc6-dev-i386_*_amd64", "aarch64": "libc6-dev_*_arm64"}
_TLS_FALLBACK = {"__libc_errno", "errno", "__libc_h_errno", "h_errno", "__h_errno", "__resp",
                 "__libc_resp", "__libc_dlerror_result", "__libc_tsd_CTYPE_B",
                 "__libc_tsd_CTYPE_TOLOWER", "__libc_tsd_CTYPE_TOUPPER", "__libc_tsd_LOCALE"}

MIN_LITERAL = 10   # see the module docstring; chosen from the ground-truth evaluation
SIG_SUBDIR = {"x86_64": "pc", "i386": "pc", "aarch64": "arm"}
ARCH_SUFFIX = {"x86_64": "amd64", "i386": "i386", "aarch64": "aarch64"}


def resolve_exc(path):
    """Resolve the collision file by excluding every conflicting module: drop the
    ';' help header so sigmake treats the file as edited, and leave the remaining
    module lines unmarked (no '+'), which excludes them. Conflicts in libc are
    overwhelmingly same-byte aliases (htons/ntohs, atoi/atol), so excluding them
    costs a couple of names while guaranteeing a clean, collision-free .sig."""
    lines = [ln for ln in open(path, "r", errors="ignore").read().splitlines()
             if not ln.startswith(";")]
    open(path, "w").write("\n".join(lines) + "\n")


def literal_bytes(line):
    """Fixed (non-wildcard) bytes a .pat line pins down: head + CRC range + tail."""
    f = line.split(" ")
    n = sum(f[0][i:i + 2] != ".." for i in range(0, len(f[0]), 2)) + int(f[1], 16)
    total = int(f[3], 16)
    tail = f[-1]
    if len(f) > 5 and re.fullmatch(r"[0-9A-F.]+", tail) and len(tail) == 2 * max(0, total - 32 - int(f[1], 16)):
        n += sum(tail[i:i + 2] != ".." for i in range(0, len(tail), 2))
    return n


def prepare_pat(src, dst, tls, arch, min_literal, relocs=True):
    """Write the pattern file sigmake will compile; returns (relaxed, dropped)."""
    relaxed = dropped = 0
    with open(src, "r", errors="ignore") as fi, open(dst, "w", newline="\n") as fo:
        for line in fi:
            line = line.rstrip("\n")
            c = False
            if tls:
                line, c = flair_relax.relax_line(line, tls, arch)
            if relocs:
                line, c2 = flair_relax.relax_relocs(line, arch)
                c = c or c2
            relaxed += c
            if min_literal and line and not line.startswith("---") and len(line.split(" ")) >= 5 \
                    and literal_bytes(line) < min_literal:
                dropped += 1
                continue
            fo.write(line + "\n")
    return relaxed, dropped


def make_one(sigmake, pat, sig, name):
    exc = os.path.splitext(sig)[0] + ".exc"
    for attempt in range(4):
        for f in (sig, exc) if attempt == 0 else (sig,):
            if os.path.exists(f):
                os.remove(f)
        subprocess.run([sigmake, "-n" + name, pat, sig],
                       capture_output=True, text=True)
        if os.path.exists(sig) and os.path.getsize(sig) > 0:
            return True
        if os.path.exists(exc):
            resolve_exc(exc)
        else:
            return False                       # failed for a non-collision reason
    return os.path.exists(sig) and os.path.getsize(sig) > 0


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("--sigmake", default=os.environ.get("FLAIR_SIGMAKE", ""))
    ap.add_argument("--db", default="_db")
    ap.add_argument("--arch", default="x86_64,i386,aarch64")
    ap.add_argument("--family", default="glibc,musl,libgcc")
    ap.add_argument("--versions", default="")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="_sigs")
    ap.add_argument("--install", default="", help="IDA install dir: copy sigs into sig/{pc,arm}")
    ap.add_argument("--cache", default="_cache", help="build-db.py cache (for libc.a TLS symbols)")
    ap.add_argument("--no-relax-tls", action="store_true",
                    help="keep raw pelf patterns (do not wildcard TLS-relaxable instruction bytes)")
    ap.add_argument("--no-relax-got", action="store_true",
                    help="keep pelf's partial wildcards on relocated instructions (see flair_relax.relax_relocs)")
    ap.add_argument("--min-literal", type=int, default=MIN_LITERAL,
                    help="drop patterns with fewer fixed bytes than this (0 = keep all; default %(default)s)")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    if not args.sigmake or not os.path.exists(args.sigmake):
        sys.exit("need --sigmake path to FLAIR sigmake(.exe)")

    want_fam = set(args.family.split(","))
    only = set(v for v in args.versions.split(",") if v)
    ok, fail, installed = [], [], 0
    for arch in args.arch.split(","):
        tls = set(_TLS_FALLBACK)
        tls |= flair_relax.tls_names(glob.glob(os.path.join(args.cache, "x", _LIBC_GLOB[arch], "libc.a")))
        pats = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(args.db, arch, "*.pat"))) +             sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(args.db, arch, "libgcc", "*.pat")))
        for pat in pats:
            key = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(pat))[0]      # "2.35", "musl_1.2.5", libgcc/"13"
            if os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(pat)) == "libgcc":
                fam, ver = "libgcc", key
            else:
                fam = "musl" if key.startswith("musl") else "glibc"
                ver = key.split("_", 1)[-1] if fam == "musl" else key
            if fam not in want_fam or (only and ver not in only):
                continue
            outdir = os.path.join(args.out, arch)
            os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
            signame = "%s_%s_%s" % (fam, ver, ARCH_SUFFIX[arch])
            sig = os.path.join(outdir, signame + ".sig")
            label = "%s %s %s static (IBR)" % (fam, ver, ARCH_SUFFIX[arch])
            tag = "%s/%s" % (arch, signame)
            src = os.path.join(outdir, signame + ".prep.pat")
            nrel, ndrop = prepare_pat(pat, src, None if args.no_relax_tls else tls, arch,
                                      args.min_literal, not args.no_relax_got)
            tag += " (relaxed %d, weak dropped %d)" % (nrel, ndrop)
            if make_one(args.sigmake, src, sig, label):
                mods = _count_modules(args.sigmake, sig)
                print("ok   %-30s %s modules" % (tag, mods)); ok.append(tag)
                if args.install:
                    dst = os.path.join(args.install, "sig", SIG_SUBDIR[arch])
                    if os.path.isdir(dst):
                        shutil.copy2(sig, dst); installed += 1
            else:
                print("FAIL %-30s" % tag); fail.append(tag)
    print("done: %d ok, %d failed, %d installed %s" % (len(ok), len(fail), installed, " ".join(fail)))
    sys.exit(1 if fail else 0)


def _count_modules(sigmake, sig):
    dumpsig = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(sigmake), "dumpsig.exe")
    if not os.path.exists(dumpsig):
        dumpsig = dumpsig[:-4]
    try:
        out = subprocess.run([dumpsig, sig], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
        m = re.search(r"(\d+) modules", out)
        return m.group(1) if m else "?"
    except Exception:
        return "?"


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/package.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Build the release zip from ida_better_reverse.py and the package in src/ibr.

The zip unpacks into the IDA user directory (or the IDA install directory):

    plugins/ida_better_reverse.py          entry point
    plugins/ida_better_reverse/ibr/...     the package, with ibr/data/
    plugins/ida_better_reverse/LICENSE

The output is reproducible: sorted entries, fixed timestamps, text files with LF endings
(a checkout with core.autocrlf=true has CRLF in the working copy).

usage: python tools/package.py [out.zip]      (default: dist/ida_better_reverse.zip)
"""
import hashlib
import os
import re
import sys
import zipfile

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
PKG = os.path.join(ROOT, "src", "ibr")
ENTRY = "ida_better_reverse.py"
FOLDER = "plugins/ida_better_reverse/"
TEXT = (".py", ".json", ".txt", ".md")


def files():
    """(path inside the zip, path on disk), sorted."""
    out = [("plugins/" + ENTRY, os.path.join(ROOT, ENTRY)),
           (FOLDER + "LICENSE", os.path.join(ROOT, "LICENSE"))]
    for dirpath, dirs, names in os.walk(PKG):
        dirs[:] = sorted(d for d in dirs if d != "__pycache__")
        for fn in sorted(names):
            if fn.endswith((".pyc", ".pyo")):
                continue
            path = os.path.join(dirpath, fn)
            rel = os.path.relpath(path, os.path.dirname(PKG)).replace(os.sep, "/")
            out.append((FOLDER + rel, path))
    return sorted(out)


def content(arc, path):
    data = open(path, "rb").read()
    if arc.endswith(TEXT) or arc.endswith("/LICENSE"):
        data = data.replace(b"\r\n", b"\n")
    if arc.endswith(".py"):
        compile(data, arc, "exec")
    return data


def main():
    out = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else os.path.join(ROOT, "dist", "ida_better_reverse.zip")
    version = re.search(r'__version__ = "([^"]+)"',
                        open(os.path.join(PKG, "__init__.py"), encoding="utf-8").read()).group(1)
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(out)), exist_ok=True)
    entries = files()
    with zipfile.ZipFile(out, "w", zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED, compresslevel=9) as z:
        for arc, path in entries:
            info = zipfile.ZipInfo(arc, date_time=(1980, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0))
            info.compress_type = zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED
            info.external_attr = 0o644 << 16
            z.writestr(info, content(arc, path))
    blob = open(out, "rb").read()
    print("files   : %d" % len(entries))
    print("wrote   : %s (%d bytes, v%s)" % (out, len(blob), version))
    print("md5     : %s" % hashlib.md5(blob).hexdigest())


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```
